# Provenance — where every file came from

meteor-cfd is source-available under the Prosperity Public License 3.0.0
(see ../LICENSE). This file is the per-file record that
[`../LICENSING.md`](../LICENSING.md) §6 phase 4 calls for: for each source
file, what it was written from.

Three categories, and nothing falls outside them:

- **literature** — implemented from the papers named in the file's own header,
  via [`SPEC-LIT.md`](SPEC-LIT.md), which carries the citations.
- **original** — designed here. No external source, permissive or otherwise.
- **format** — reads or writes the OpenFOAM ASCII case format. A file format is
  not a work of authorship, and interoperability is the whole purpose.
- **carried over** — moved across from this project's own earlier C++ when the
  crate became Rust, rather than rewritten. See the note below; this matters,
  and it is why the category exists.

**No file in this tree was written from GPL-licensed source.** The numerical
core that was is gone; see `LICENSING.md` for what was removed and why.

## What "carried over" means, and why it is not a hole in the audit

The crate was Rust-with-CUDA before the relicensing rewrite, and it had been
ported from an earlier C++ version of the same project. When the GPL-derived
numerics were deleted and rewritten, three groups of files were **not**
rewritten, because nothing in them was derived from anyone else's code:

| Carried over | What it does |
|---|---|
| `src/io/*` | reads and writes the ASCII case format |
| `src/blockgen.rs` | generates structured meshes and writes them in that format |
| `src/bin/*` | argument parsing, case loading, reporting loops |

Their headers used to say *"Transcribed from `gpu/common/io/foam_io.cu`"*, and
one said the deleted C++ was *"the specification"* — wording that is accurate
about the mechanics, useless to a reader now that the path is gone, and
alarming in exactly the way the numerics turned out to deserve. They now state
the real chain: the C++ they came from was itself written from the case format
as it appears in data files, not from any CFD code's source.

The distinction that matters: the deleted C++ had its **numerics** transcribed
from OpenFOAM. Its **I/O layer and mesh generator** were not — they were
written from data files, which is why they are here and the numerics are not.
This paragraph exists because an audit of this repository flagged the
contradiction between the old headers and this document. The headers were
wrong; the classification was right.

---

The device twin (§100.9) makes §100.1's two stated choices exactly as the host evaluator does and is held to it to 1e-12; it is written from SPEC-LIT and from nothing else. No GPL-licensed source was consulted |

## The low-Mach loop — literature

`ofgpu-lowmach`'s variable-density formulation: SPEC-LIT §25 and §26, and the
§13.4 selector that names the radiation models.

| File | SPEC-LIT | Primary sources |
|---|---|---|
| `src/energy.rs`, `cuda/energy.cu` | §25, §26, §77.6, §93.5 | Rehm & Baum, *J. Res. Natl. Bur. Stand.* 83 (1978) 297-308 (the `p = p0(t) + p~` split, the divergence constraint and `p0` evolution); Majda & Sethian, *Combust. Sci. Technol.* 42 (1985) 185 (low-Mach acoustic filtering); the FDS Technical Reference Guide (McGrattan et al., NIST SP 1018, public domain — `reference/fds` read and adapted for the SHAPE of the divergence constraint and the sealed/open `p0` bookkeeping, acknowledged per SPEC-LIT §0; no FDS code copied); Patankar (1980) §4.2 for the `Su`/`Sp` linearisation the `EnergySources` registry hands to `fvm_su`/`fvm_sp`. **Kays, *ASME J. Heat Transfer* 116 (1994) 284-295** and **Kays & Crawford, *Convective Heat and Mass Transfer*, 4th ed., ch. 13** now DRIVE code: SPEC-LIT §37's variable turbulent Prandtl number (`PrtModel`, `kays_crawford_prt`, kernel `energyKEffKaysCrawford`), the model behind `k_eff = k + rho cp nu_t/Pr_t(Pe_t)`. *Until §37 they were cited by SPEC-LIT §32.5.5 and `docs/07-lowmach-solver.md` §1.1 and implemented by nothing - the reference behind an unmeasured hypothesis. The hypothesis has since been measured; see `docs/07-lowmach-solver.md` §1.1's last subsection for what it found* — **and SPEC-LIT §26.1** adds Moukalled, Mangani & Darwish (2016) §12.4 and §15.4, Patankar (1980) §5.2, and Ferziger & Perić, *Computational Methods for Fluid Dynamics*, 3rd ed. (2002), §5.3, for the conservative/non-conservative pair and the rule that a scalar equation's convecting flux must satisfy the discrete continuity its non-conservative form assumes. That is the section that completed §25.1's `Q` with its conduction term `div(k_eff grad T)` (`Energy::update_conduction_source`), formed with this crate's own `fv::sn_grad_flux`/`fv::fvc_div_surface` off the same face flux `fvm_laplacian` assembles — no new discretisation, no external source |
| `src/radiation.rs` | §13.4, §49, §50 | ORIGINAL. The Stefan-Boltzmann constant is CODATA 2018's exact value, fixed by the SI redefinition of the kelvin and therefore not transcribed from a measurement; the `radiationModel` name gate, its three names and the two refusals it dispatches between are this project's own §13.4 contract. It carries no solver, no kernel and no discretisation. Split out of the row below so that the selector every radiation model shares does not sit inside one of the models it selects |

## Lagrangian parcels — literature

The dispersed phase: SPEC-LIT §66. **One-way coupled and inert** — parcels feel
the gas, the gas does not feel them, and nothing here writes a cell field or a
matrix entry, so every Eulerian result in the crate is unmoved by construction
rather than by measurement.

| File | SPEC-LIT | Primary sources |
|---|---|---|
| `src/parcels.rs`, `src/parcels/tests.rs`, `cuda/parcels.cu` | §66 | **Dukowicz, *J. Comput. Phys.* 35 (1980) 229-253**, DOI `10.1016/0021-9991(80)90087-X` — the discrete droplet model: (66.1)'s ensemble of delta functions and the real-valued parcel weight `n_p`. **Crowe, Sommerfeld & Tsuji, *Multiphase Flows with Droplets and Particles*, CRC Press (1998)** — equation of motion (66.2) and the regime argument for which terms survive at `rho/rho_l ~ 1e-3`; **Maxey & Riley, *Phys. Fluids* 26 (1983) 883** — the derivation behind `C_am = 1/2`. **Schiller & Naumann**, *Z. Ver. Deutsch. Ing.* 77 (1933) 318, in the compilation of **Clift, Grace & Weber, *Bubbles, Drops, and Particles*, Academic Press (1978)** — the drag correlation (66.5). **The singularity-free form (66.6) — `K = rho C_d |u_rel|` rather than `C_d` — is DERIVED here**, and so is the `expm1`/`(1-e^-b)/b` form (66.8) of the exponential update and the closed-form terminal balance (66.7) with its `C_am` cancellation; none of the three is transcribed from anywhere. **`reference/fds/` (McGrattan et al., NIST SP 1018-1, US-Government PUBLIC DOMAIN — its `LICENSE.md` read verbatim in the local clone this repository does not carry)**, chapter "Lagrangian Particles" and appendix "Fluid-Particle Momentum Transfer" — read for the EXPONENTIAL integration of the linearised drag (66.8), the trapezoidal position update (66.9) and the sub-step CFL bound (66.11). FDS is CPU/MPI Fortran on a Cartesian mesh and answers no GPU question: the SoA pool, the CSR walk, the fixed-geometry grid-stride kernels reading a device-resident count, and the bijective identity are this project's own. **Macpherson, Nordin & Weller, *Commun. Numer. Meth. Engng* 25 (2009) 263**, DOI `10.1002/cnm.1128` — barycentric tracking on a tet decomposition, **NAMED IN §66.6 AS THE FIX AND NOT IMPLEMENTED**; the **paper** was read and **OpenFOAM's implementation of it is GPL-3.0 and was NOT opened**. **Steele, Lea & Flood, OOPSLA 2014, *ACM SIGPLAN Notices* 49(10) 453**, DOI `10.1145/2660193.2660195` — SplitMix64's finalising mix, whose two multiplier constants are the published ones and whose reference implementation (Vigna's `splitmix64.c`) is **public domain, CC0**. It is used in (66.14) as a **bijection**, not as a generator: that is what makes parcel identity unique by construction rather than by a birthday argument, and `the_uid_mix_is_a_bijection` inverts it to prove it. Named and **NOT implemented**, each with a section number: Ranz & Marshall, *Chem. Eng. Prog.* 48 (1952) 141 and 173, and Sazhin, *Prog. Energy Combust. Sci.* 32 (2006) 162 — evaporation, refused by name here and **IMPLEMENTED at §76**, which is why `ParcelPhysics` now has three values; Abramzon & Sirignano, *Int. J. Heat Mass Transfer* 32 (1989) 1605 — its successor; Bai & Gosman, SAE 950283 (1995) and Mundo, Sommerfeld & Tropea, *Int. J. Multiphase Flow* 21 (1995) 151 — the four-outcome wall map, of which `stick`, `spread`, `film` and `splash` were **REFUSED BY NAME WITH THE REASON** here (§66.10) and which is **IMPLEMENTED at §78**, so `stick` is a value now and `weber` selects among all four; `film` and `splash` are still refused and carry two DIFFERENT reasons (§78.11, §78.7); Chan, *J. Fire Prot. Eng.* 6(2) (1994) 79 and Yu, IAFSS 1 (1986) 1165 — the Rosin-Rammler/log-normal size distribution and `D_v50 ~ We^(-1/3)`, unreachable until an RNG exists; Salmon, Moraes, Dror & Shaw, SC '11 article 16 — the counter-based (Philox/Threefry) generator §66.8 names as the prerequisite for all of them, whose reference implementation is BSD-3-Clause and was **not** vendored; Subramaniam, *Prog. Energy Combust. Sci.* 39 (2013) 215 — the three-way convergence statement of §66.1; Elghobashi, *Appl. Sci. Res.* 52 (1994) 309 — the coupling-regime map that says when one-way coupling stops being enough. **NVIDIA cuda-samples (BSD-3-Clause) and AMReX (BSD-3-Clause, LBNL)** were identified as the permissively-licensed prior art for GPU particle containers and sort-by-cell-hash; **neither is used here**, because both deposit through floating-point atomics and this crate forbids them — the fact that the closest permissive prior art is a counterexample on the one property that matters is recorded in §66.14 rather than glossed. **PyFR (BSD-3-Clause) has no Lagrangian particle module at all.** **No GPL-licensed source was consulted**, and in particular **OpenFOAM's `src/lagrangian` tree — the obvious reference for every line of this — was not opened**, nor SU2 (LGPL), nor PySDM/libcloudph++/LIGGGHTS/SPARTA (all GPL) |

## The parcel sort and gather-shaped deposition — literature

Turning the per-cell sum of the PSI-Cell construction from a **scatter** into a
**gather**: SPEC-LIT §67. Still one-way coupled — nothing here writes a
momentum, energy or species source — but the machinery that will carry them is
here, and its whole reason for existing is that the obvious implementation
(`atomicAdd(double*)`) destroys the bitwise reproducibility this crate is
organised around.

| File | SPEC-LIT | Primary sources |
|---|---|---|
| `src/parcels/deposit.rs`, `src/parcels/deposit/tests.rs`, `cuda/parcelsort.cu` | §67 | **Crowe, Sharma & Stock, *J. Fluids Eng.* 99 (1977) 325**, DOI `10.1115/1.3448756` — the particle-source-in-cell construction, equation (67.1): a cell quantity is the sum over the parcels in the cell of `n_p` times the per-droplet quantity, over the cell volume. **Satish, Harris & Garland, IEEE IPDPS 2009**, DOI `10.1109/IPDPS.2009.5161005` — the three-phase radix pass of (67.6)/(67.7): a per-block digit histogram, ONE global exclusive scan over digit-major block counters, and a stable scatter whose destination is a closed form. The **paper** was read; **no implementation of it was opened**, and CUB — whose device-wide radix sort is the obvious call — is unreachable from this build because `build.rs` emits CUBIN with no host translation unit (§67.8(a)). **Merrill & Grimshaw, University of Virginia Technical Report CS2009-14** — the reduce-then-scan decomposition of (67.3); decoupled look-back was **rejected** because it needs an atomically assigned tile order, which is the scheduling dependence §67 exists to remove. **Blelloch, CMU-CS-90-190 (1990)** — the exclusive scan and its work-efficiency argument. **Hillis & Steele, *Commun. ACM* 29(12) (1986) 1170**, DOI `10.1145/7902.7903` — the in-block scan network, chosen *against* work-efficiency because its shape is a function of `blockDim` alone and is therefore deterministic by construction. **Elghobashi, *Appl. Sci. Res.* 52 (1994) 309**, DOI `10.1007/BF00936835` — the coupling-regime map that says what the deposited `alpha_p` of (67.9) is for. **The canonicalisation argument of §67.1 is ORIGINAL**: that sorting on `(cell, uid)` makes the CSR a pure function of the physical state, hence of neither slot order nor injection order nor block count, and that the gate for it must therefore permute the slots while holding the parcel SET fixed. So is the `nCells` sentinel of (67.5), whose lower bound *is* the live count; so is the binary-search CSR of (67.8), which needs no atomic and no scan; and so is the fixed-at-setup ping-pong parity of §67.7, without which a captured graph and the host disagree about which buffer holds the answer. **NVIDIA cuda-samples (BSD-3-Clause) and AMReX (BSD-3-Clause, LBNL)** remain the closest permissive prior art for GPU particle containers and sort-by-cell-hash, and remain **unused for the same reason as in §66**: both deposit through floating-point atomics. **No GPL-licensed source was consulted**, and in particular **OpenFOAM's `src/lagrangian` tree — which contains the obvious reference implementation of a per-cell parcel grouping — was not opened** |

## Two-way coupling of the dispersed phase — literature

Giving the gas back what the parcels took: SPEC-LIT §68. The momentum and
sensible-heat sources, through §18's registries, so that nothing in the solvers
learns that parcels exist. Evaporation, droplet radiation and wall splash are
**refused by name** here rather than approximated.

| File | SPEC-LIT | Primary sources |
|---|---|---|
| `src/parcels/couple.rs`, `src/parcels/couple/tests.rs`, `cuda/parcelcouple.cu` | §68, §77 | **Crowe, Sharma & Stock, *J. Fluids Eng.* 99 (1977) 325**, DOI `10.1115/1.3448756` — the particle-source-in-cell construction (68.1), now evaluated for real. **Patankar, *Numerical Heat Transfer and Fluid Flow*, Hemisphere (1980), §4.2, §7.2** — the `S_u + S_p psi` linearisation and the rule `S_p <= 0`, which (68.10) satisfies **by construction**: `beta_P` is a sum of `n_p m_eff (1 - e^-x)` with `x >= 0`, so no clamp and no sign branch exists in the kernel. **Ranz & Marshall, *Chem. Eng. Prog.* 48 (1952) 141 and 173** — `Nu = 2 + 0.6 Re^(1/2) Pr^(1/3)`; only its **sensible-heat half** is implemented here (68.8); the evaporation the same papers give was refused by name at §68.13 and **IMPLEMENTED at §76**, and §68's refusal of a mass COUPLING survives with a new reason. **Maxey & Riley, *Phys. Fluids* 26 (1983) 883**, DOI `10.1063/1.864230` — the equation of motion whose drag term is the one being returned to the gas. **Elghobashi, *Appl. Sci. Res.* 52 (1994) 309**, DOI `10.1007/BF00936835` — the coupling map that says when two-way coupling is required at all. **`reference/fds/` (McGrattan et al., NIST, US-Government PUBLIC DOMAIN — its `LICENSE.md` read verbatim in the local clone this repository does not carry)**: the Technical Reference Guide's appendix *Fluid-Particle Momentum Transfer* for the principle that the gas receives the negative of the droplets' own momentum change, and `Validation/Theobald_Hose_Stream/FDS_Input_Files/Build_Input_Files/{paramfile.csv, build_input_files.py}` — **input decks and a data-generation script, which are data, not solver source** — for the transcription of §68.12's 90 experiments and for the derivations they document (efflux velocity `3.71 sqrt(dP[psi])`, droplet diameter one tenth of the bore, `PRIMARY_BREAKUP_LENGTH` as twice Theobald's Eq. 2). **Theobald, *Fire Safety Journal* 4 (1981) 1-13** — the hose-stream experiments themselves, which §68.12 reports against and **MISSES**, with the miss and its diagnosis on `ofgpu-validate`'s own screen. **The impulse formulation of §68.1 is ORIGINAL to this section**: that the conservative source is the impulse the exponential integrator ALREADY applied, `m_eff (Delta u_p - a_g h)`, rather than the re-linearised `(m_p/tau_p)(u_p - u)` the design note recommends — which is cheaper, exact through sub-steps, cell crossings and wall rebounds, and turns the conservation gate from a tolerance into an identity. So is the observation of §68.3 that the empty-cell deposit is `-0.0` and that `-0.0` is the additive identity that leaves every accumulator bitwise unmoved. So is the argument of §68.4 for a second whole-field registry rather than a `SourceTerm` variant, and the placement of the coherent core of §68.12 as a closed-form vacuum parabola that needs no kernel at all. **SPEC-LIT §77** adds, on the same kernel and the same CSR walk: **Rehm & Baum, *J. Res. NBS* 83 (1978) 297-308** — the low-Mach divergence constraint the vapour's own volume enters; the **FDS Technical Reference Guide**'s chapter *The Divergence* (NIST SP 1018-1, US-Government PUBLIC DOMAIN, `reference/fds`) — the same `D_SOURCE` term in its general variable-molar-mass form, of which §25's constant-`W` gas keeps `mdot/rho` and nothing else, which §77.6 states rather than leaving to be inferred; and the **ASHRAE Handbook — Fundamentals (2017), ch. 1, eq. 33** — the adiabatic-saturation relation gate 77-D is measured against, reached through this crate's own §54 (`psychro::t_wb`) rather than re-fitted. **ORIGINAL to §77**: the finding that there is NO second latent-heat sink — the droplet's budget (76.10) puts every joule the phase change consumed inside the convective heat the gas has already given up, so depositing `q_lat` again would double-count it, and what the gas is actually owed is the arriving mass's sensible enthalpy `cp_g mdot (T_p - T_g)`, 12 % of the latent heat rather than 100 % of it (§77.4); the statement of §77.6 that the ENERGY half of evaporation's effect on the divergence arrives through §25.1's `Q` with no code at all while the VOLUME half does not, which is §26.1's omission with the halves swapped; the additive `update_target_divergence_with`, which leaves `energyTargetDivergence` and its caller untouched so that a run with no dispersed phase is bitwise what it was BY CONSTRUCTION; and the observation that `mdot` is not sign-definite — a droplet in supersaturated air grows — so neither §77 source can be made an implicit sink without the first clamp in this addition (§77.5). **No GPL-licensed source was consulted**, and in particular **OpenFOAM's `src/lagrangian` tree — which contains the obvious reference implementation of a parcel-to-cell source — was not opened**, nor SU2 (LGPL) |
| `src/parcels/evaporation.rs`, `src/parcels/evaporation/tests.rs` (the evaporation half of `cuda/parcels.cu`) | §76 | **Ranz & Marshall, *Chem. Eng. Prog.* 48 (1952) 141-146 and 173-180** — `Nu_0 = 2 + 0.6 Re^(1/2) Pr^(1/3)`, `Sh_0 = 2 + 0.6 Re^(1/2) Sc^(1/3)`, and the `d(D^2)/dt` metric of §76.12's first gate. The papers whose sensible-heat half §68.8 implemented and whose mass-transfer half §68.13 refused by name; this section is the other half, so the refusal list shrank by one. **Spalding, 4th Symp. (Int.) Combust. (1953) 847-864**, and *Convective Mass Transfer: An Introduction*, Edward Arnold (1963) — the mass transfer number `B_M` and the Stefan-flow rate `mdot = -pi d rho_f D_f Sh_0 ln(1 + B_M)`. **Godsave, 4th Symp. (Int.) Combust. (1953) 818-830** — the heat-limited rate of the boiling branch (76.9), which is what is physically true exactly where the diffusion-limited rate diverges. **Abramzon & Sirignano, *Int. J. Heat Mass Transfer* 32 (1989) 1605-1618**, DOI `10.1016/0017-9310(89)90043-4` — `B_T = (1 + B_M)^phi - 1` with `phi = (c_pv/c_pg)(Sh_0/Nu_0)/Le`, the DEFAULT here because `phi ~ 2.07` for water in air and `phi = 1` is what FDS's simplification assumes; their film-thickness corrections `Nu*`/`Sh*` are **NAMED AND REFUSED** (§76.6), because a fixed point is a data-dependent trip count and a captured graph cannot hold one. **Sazhin, *Prog. Energy Combust. Sci.* 32 (2006) 162-214**, DOI `10.1016/j.pecs.2005.11.001` — the modern re-derivation and the survey of which successors earn their flops; its effective-conductivity droplet-interior model is named as what a `Bi ~ 0.1` fuel droplet would need and is **NOT implemented** (§76.8). **Watson, *Ind. Eng. Chem.* 35 (1943) 398-406** — `h_v(T) = h_v(T_b)[(T_c - T)/(T_c - T_b)]^0.38`, whose 1.33 % high reading for water at 25 C is measured and printed rather than absorbed. **Marrero & Mason, *J. Phys. Chem. Ref. Data* 1 (1972) 3-118**, DOI `10.1063/1.3253094` — `D(H2O-air) = 1.87e-10 T^2.072/p[atm]` m²/s over 282-450 K, which sets the default diffusivity, its temperature exponent, and (through the Lewis number) the size of §76.13's wet-bulb gap; the widely quoted `2.42e-5` is 3.6 % away and §76.13 states what choosing it would move. **Hyland & Wexler, *ASHRAE Transactions* 89(2A) (1983) 500-519** — the saturation polynomial, taken from this crate's own §54 (`src/psychro.rs`) rather than re-fitted, so that the two modules cannot disagree about water; its analytic `d(ln p_ws)/dT`, which the device linearisation needs, is **DERIVED here** and checked against a central difference of §54's own function. **Lewis, *Trans. ASME* 44 (1922) 325-340** — the heat/mass transfer coefficient relation whose failure at `Le != 1` is what §76.13's gap is made of. **NIST Chemistry WebBook, SRD 69 (US-Government PUBLIC DOMAIN)** — the water-vapour specific heat and the critical constants. **`reference/fds/` (McGrattan et al., NIST SP 1018-1, US-Government PUBLIC DOMAIN — its `LICENSE.md` read verbatim in the local clone this repository does not carry)**, chapter "Lagrangian Particles" and appendix "Development of an Implicit Solution for Droplet Evaporation" — read for the model set; its `B_T = B_M` simplification is offered as `massTransfer spalding` and is **not** the default, and its cell-coupled 3x3 implicit closure is **deliberately not reproduced**, because this section holds the gas frozen and the ordering hazard that closure exists to manage does not arise (§76). **ORIGINAL to this section**: the ordering of (76.10), in which the mass is DERIVED from the energy the exponential integrator actually applied rather than integrated beside it — §68's construction one equation over, and what makes the droplet's own budget `C dT = Q_c - dm h_v` an f64 identity; the factored endpoint difference `rho_l (pi/6)(d_0 - d)(d_0^2 + d_0 d + d^2)` of (76.11), which is exact where `d_0^3 - d^3` loses four orders of magnitude to cancellation; the PINNING of `T_p` at `T_b` in the boiling branch, without which the two branches alternate on a one-ulp comparison and the evaporation rate collapses by ten orders of magnitude — a defect the boiling gate caught; the vaporisation of arrival superheat rather than its deletion; the scan-and-bisect boiling-point solve, forced by the finding that Clausius-Clapeyron with Watson's `h_v` is **non-monotone** above ~545 K for water and returns `p_b` again at the critical point; and the evaporation sub-step bound (76.12). **No GPL-licensed source was consulted**, and in particular **OpenFOAM's `src/lagrangian` tree — which contains the obvious reference implementation of a droplet evaporation model — was not opened**, nor SU2 (LGPL), nor PySDM (GPL-3.0) |
| `src/parcels/impact.rs`, `src/parcels/impact/tests.rs` (the impact half of `cuda/parcels.cu`) | §78 | **C. Bai & A. D. Gosman, SAE Technical Paper 950283 (1995)**, DOI `10.4271/950283` — **PAYWALLED and NOT obtained**; the constants below are the ones its citing literature quotes, which §78.10 and §78.12 both state, and which is the reason every one of them is a CONTROL with a default rather than a constant in the source (§60.5's Kaminski & Prakash row is the precedent for saying so). The four-regime dry-wall impingement map (adhesion / rebound / spread / splash), its Weber-number boundaries `We ~ 2` and `We ~ 20`, and the splash threshold `We_c = A La^(-0.18)` whose `A` is roughness-dependent and is therefore the control `splashA` rather than a constant. It was **named and NOT implemented** at §66.10, where all four of its outcomes were refused; §78 implements the map, so the refusal list shrank by one and the two that remain (`film`, `splash`) carry two DIFFERENT reasons. **C. Mundo, M. Sommerfeld & C. Tropea, *Int. J. Multiphase Flow* 21 (1995) 151-173**, DOI `10.1016/0301-9322(94)00069-V` — the deposition/splashing parameter `K = Oh Re^1.25` and its measured threshold `K = 57.7`, which is the DEFAULT criterion. Their splashing data themselves are **NOT transcribed** and gate 78-D says so: the paper's boundary is a figure, not a table, and §78.10 records that this section has no experimental gate. **A. L. Yarin, *Annu. Rev. Fluid Mech.* 38 (2006) 159-192**, DOI `10.1146/annurev.fluid.38.050304.092144` — the review behind §78.1's statement that neither correlation is better than approximately right, which is why every threshold here is a control. **IAPWS, *Revised Release on Surface Tension of Ordinary Water Substance*, R1-76 (2014)** — `sigma = B tau^mu (1 + b tau)` with `B = 235.8 mN/m`, `b = -0.625`, `mu = 1.256`; a freely published international standard, **implemented and gated against the release's own table** (worst 0.0045 mN/m over 0-300 °C), and the source of the default `sigma` at 293.15 K. **NIST Chemistry WebBook, SRD 69 (US Government, PUBLIC DOMAIN)** — the liquid viscosity of water that sets the default `muLiquid`. Named and **NOT implemented**, each with a section number: **P. J. O'Rourke & A. A. Amsden, SAE 961961 (1996)** and **SAE 2000-01-0271 (2000)**, and **D. W. Stanton & C. J. Rutland, *Int. J. Heat Mass Transfer* 41 (1998) 3037**, DOI `10.1016/S0017-9310(98)00054-4` — the wall-film momentum equation, refused by name in §78.11; the FDS Technical Reference Guide's much cheaper fixed-dripping-velocity film (NIST SP 1018-1, US-Government PUBLIC DOMAIN, `reference/fds`), which needs the RNG §66.8 also does not have; and Bai & Gosman's own STOCHASTIC retained-mass fraction, which §78.7 declines for the same reason and because deleting the un-retained part would break the ledger's exact partition. **ORIGINAL to this section**: the fourth-power decision form `K^4 = We^2 Re` of (78.4b) — an identity, because `Oh Re = sqrt(We)` — which takes Mundo's decision with no `pow` and no `sqrt` and is therefore bit-stable across compute capabilities where the criterion's own form is not (§38.6); the closed-form inverses (78.4c) that make the boundary gate analytic rather than a bisection against the kernel; the observation that a deposit is `remove` plus exactly TWO statements, because §66's pool reclaims no slot and therefore already holds the arriving droplet's diameter and weight; and the resulting ledger (78.6), which is a PARTITION of the pool rather than an accumulator — no atomic, floating-point or otherwise, and "nothing vanishes at a wall" is a statement about bits rather than a tolerance. **No GPL-licensed source was consulted**, and in particular **OpenFOAM's `src/lagrangian/intermediate/submodels/.../PatchInteractionModel` tree — which contains the obvious reference implementation of a Bai-Gosman patch interaction — was not opened**, nor SU2 (LGPL), nor KIVA |

## New I/O formats and machinery — format / literature

| File | What it is |
|---|---|
| `src/io/msh.rs` | Gmsh `.msh` 4.1 reader (tet/hex/prism/pyramid cells, `$PhysicalNames` patches). The FORMAT is published (Gmsh manual) and not copyrightable; `reference/pyfr`'s BSD-3 `pyfr/readers/gmsh.py` was the legal cross-check for blocked `$Nodes`, non-contiguous tags and physical names — no Gmsh source was read |
| `src/io/regions.rs`, `src/io/regions/tests.rs`, `src/bin/regions.rs` | The region layout of `docs/10-fsi-solid-mesh-plan.md` §C (`regions.json` + one polyMesh per region, SPEC-LIT §97) — ORIGINAL manifest format; the `cellZones` reader (`polymesh::read_cell_zones`) reads a published case-format file learned from its shape; the splitter and `ofgpu-regions` are original. OpenFOAM's `splitMeshRegions`, `cellZones` and `polyMesh` classes are GPL and were not opened. No GPL-licensed source was consulted |
| `src/surface/stl.rs`, `src/surface/obj.rs` | STL (3D Systems, 1987, a de facto public spec) and Wavefront OBJ (also a published, uncopyrightable format) readers, for surface intake (SPEC-LIT §23) |
| `src/surface/classify.rs` | Column-parity ray-casting inside/outside classification (SPEC-LIT §23.3); Barill, Dickson, Schmidt, Levin & Jacobson, *ACM TOG* 37(4) (2018), the exact solid-angle winding number as tie-break |
| `src/surface/cutcell.rs`, the cut-cell section of `src/blockgen.rs` | Embedded-boundary volume/area fractions by supersampling (SPEC-LIT §24) — Aftosmis, Berger & Melton, *AIAA J.* 36(6) (1998) 952 for the castellation/cut-cell context; the cut face's closure-defined area vector, the merge rule and the sample count are *DESIGN* (§24.3, §24.5) |
| `src/restart.rs` | The `.mcr` restart file: full-precision `phi`, mesh-hash guarded, versioned. Format and layout are *DESIGN* — original, no external spec |
| `src/io/case_json.rs` | The JSONC case reader/lowering (`docs/05-io-redesign.md` §4.1). `schemars`-generated JSON Schema so the schema and the reader cannot disagree; the patch-major, ordered-array layout is prior art from `reference/pyfr`'s `[soln-bcs-<patch>]` INI convention (BSD-3), read as a case-format precedent, not code |
| `src/io/vtu.rs` | Appended-binary VTU/VTK writer; `reference/pyfr`'s BSD-3 `pyfr/writers/vtk/` was the cross-check for the encoding and offsets; `write_vtu_points` writes the polyMesh's own polygons (each face wound outward for the cell that emits it), a `PointData` block and nine-component `Tensors` from `PolyMeshRaw` - the same published Kitware format description, no new format source consulted |
| `src/io/pointfield.rs` | Cell-to-point inverse-distance interpolation and the point/cell adjacency behind the `PointData` block of `io::vtu` (planned as part of §93; the file itself cites only §1, §3.5, §49.3). **The inverse-distance weight of SPEC-LIT §3.5** (`w = 1/|d|`; Jasak (1996) §3.3.2, Moukalled et al. (2016) §9.3), applied over the cells sharing a point instead of over a cell's neighbours - ORIGINAL beyond that, no external spec. The adjacency is built from `PolyMeshRaw`, which the caller keeps (SPEC-LIT §49.3's pattern: `HostMesh` is not extended). **OpenFOAM's `volPointInterpolation` is GPL and was not opened.** No GPL-licensed source was consulted |
| `src/io/vdb.rs`, `src/io/nvdb.rs` | OpenVDB-compatible and NanoVDB volume writers, `fp32` and `fp16` (SPEC-LIT §45). The NanoVDB layout is read from the Apache-2.0 PNanoVDB/NanoVDB headers (AcademySoftwareFoundation GitHub); the `.vdb` layout, including the `_HalfFloat` save preference, from the Apache-2.0 OpenVDB source at `v13.0.0`, cited file by file inline |
| `src/io/output_plan.rs` | The `output` block of the case format, resolved and driven (SPEC-LIT §44) — ORIGINAL, no external spec |
| `src/io/usda.rs` | USD ASCII (`.usda`) scene writer referencing the VDB output — the format is Pixar's published USD spec |

## Ours by design

Marked *DESIGN* in `SPEC-LIT.md` where the literature does not prescribe the
choice. These are documented as our decisions in the code, not attributed to
anyone.

| Thing | Where | SPEC-LIT |
|---|---|---|
| The single Robin triple `(fr, ref_value, ref_grad)` for every BC | `src/field.rs`, `src/field_setup.rs` | §4 |
| Vertex welding by exact bit-equality only — no epsilon weld, because an epsilon is a silent geometry edit | `src/surface/mod.rs`, `src/surface/stl.rs` | §23.1 |
| Faces carved against solid cells are `wall` type and receive the same wall boundary conditions blockgen already writes, so a carved case runs unmodified | `src/blockgen.rs` | §23.4 |
| Gather over a cell→face CSR instead of scatter over faces | `src/mesh/topology.rs` and every kernel | §1 |
| Ordering a row of `A·psi` by the **global** face id rather than the local one, in one merged list with a two-bit flags word, so that the summation order is a property of the mesh and not of how the mesh was cut up. The identity map puts every internal face's key below every boundary face's, so on an undecomposed mesh the merged list is bit-for-bit the two old lists concatenated and no default can move — proved by construction, then measured (`ofgpu-validate` and every field the shipped cases write, unchanged to the byte). Applied to the four kernels that accumulate internal AND boundary faces into one register (`solAmul`, `lduAmul`, `lduRelax`, `lduSetValues`) and deliberately **not** to the 16 assembly and gradient kernels §70.5 enumerates, whose cut-face coefficients are computed by different arithmetic on the two sides and which an ordering therefore cannot fix. Costs ~52 B/cell, added and not recovered | `src/mesh/topology.rs`, `src/mesh.rs`, `cuda/ldu.cu`, `cuda/solver.cu` | §70 |
| Accumulating a cross-part reduction in INTEGER limbs against one GLOBAL anchor rather than combining per-part floating-point partials, so the answer is a function of the multiset of terms and not of the cut. `W = 30` and `K = 4` rather than the literature's `W = 40`, `K = 3`: at 30 bits a limb cannot overflow an `i64` for any mesh a 32-bit label can index, which deletes the carry-normalisation pass instead of scheduling one, and 120 binades is already 67 below the last bit a double can hold. The anchor is a GLOBAL maximum and is deliberately NOT fused into the limb collective - a per-part anchor truncates each part's terms at a different place and the partition invariance is gone, which is a **correction to the design note this section was written against**. Both constructions are implemented and measured side by side: the gathered per-part partial (run-invariant, four lines) moved in 20 of 108 relabelled decompositions on the solver's own output and 75 of 108 on an adversarial field, the exact accumulator in none. **Nothing calls it yet**, and §72.6's measured 4.1-5.0x is why that is a decision rather than a deferral | `src/exactsum.rs`, `cuda/exactsum.cu` | §72 |
| Running PCG and PBiCGStab over a decomposition by putting the halo exchange in **one place only** - immediately before the matrix product, which is the only operator in either recurrence whose stencil leaves a cell - and substituting the cross-part reduction for the local one, so that the recurrences themselves are the serial solver's kernels unchanged. Both invariance halves are made one predicate in code (`DistWorkspace::partition_invariant`) rather than a comment: the exact accumulator AND a preconditioner a cut cannot change. **The block-local DIC/DILU is named block Jacobi, not restricted additive Schwarz** - a correction to the design note this section was written against, since the halo is read by `lduAmul` and by nothing in `precon.rs`, so the overlap RAS restricts does not exist here (Saad 2003 ch. 14; Cai & Sarkis 1999 cited to be distinguished from). `normFactor`'s mean divides by the GLOBAL cell count, which closes the `sum(b)/n` hazard §72.7 left open. Measured rather than argued: the diagonal preconditioner degrades by exactly nothing at every part count, DIC/DILU by 0.91x-1.84x at P = 16 over the ten rows of five meshes (nine a penalty of 1.03x-1.84x, one a speed-up), and the degradation is a step function of which direction the cut crosses rather than a smooth function of P. Gated on the shipped cases at 78/78 bitwise-and-same-iteration under three partitioners, and at 156/156 again under every rotation of the part labels. **Nothing calls it yet** | `src/distsolve.rs` | §73 |
| Continuous blending across `y+_lam`, and the wall-adjacent-cell treatment | `src/wallfunctions.rs` | §6.4 |
| The skewness vector `s_f = Cf - (C_P + (1-w)d)` written from the interpolation WEIGHT rather than from a second projection, so the correction and the interpolation it corrects cannot disagree about where the uncorrected value was placed — plus a `1e-9 \|d\|` FLOOR below which the vector is zeroed. The floor was forced by a measurement, not chosen: `s_f` is a difference of two nearly equal COMPUTED positions, so when the true value is zero the computed one has no significant digits, and a uniform box came out at `6.9e-18` on some faces and exactly zero on others depending on its cell dimensions. Without it, "an unskewed mesh gives the same bits" would be a property of the numbers in the case file — this project's own first version of the test passed on one box and failed on another. Real skewness at a 2:1 interface is `0.1421`, eight orders above the floor; centroid noise on a mesh a thousand cells across is four orders below it | `src/mesh/geometry.rs`, `src/mesh.rs` | §2.5, §74.4 |
| Measuring the 2:1-interface convergence gate in **L-infinity as well as L2** — and the finding that L2 alone would have passed the UNMODIFIED code (1.985) while L-infinity shows it stalling dead (−0.070). A refinement interface is a two-dimensional set inside a three-dimensional mesh, so a volume-weighted norm gives a local defect there only its own share; the gate as originally briefed could have been met by doing nothing. This is the correction to the brief that mattered | `src/fv.rs` (`a_statically_refined_mesh_recovers_second_order`) | §74.5, §74.6 |
| The load-bearing piece at a 2:1 interface is the GRADIENT, not the snGrad scheme: Green-Gauss on the fine side is wrong at ZEROTH order (30.17 % of the gradient on the gate mesh, and it does not shrink with h), and that error is fed straight into §2.4's correction. `leastSquares`, which this crate already shipped, is exact for a linear field on ANY mesh and reaches L-infinity order 1.931 with no new code — recorded as the recommendation rather than buried, because it makes most of §74.4's second half optional | `src/fv.rs`, `cuda/fv.cu` | §74.6 |
| The skewness correction of the Green-Gauss gradient is a PICARD iteration, not a one-shot: one pass corrects the face value with a gradient that is itself uncorrected. Measured contraction 0.221, converging to `2.3e-15` on a linear field — stated with the ladder (0 / 1 / 2 / converged passes) rather than shipped as if one pass were exact | `src/fv.rs`, `cuda/fv.cu` | §74.4 |
| `ofgpu-validate` deliberately does NOT run `check_explicit_operators` on the refined mesh: two of its checks are FALSE there, by 30.17 % and 1.20 %, and that is what §74.4 exists to fix rather than a bug in them. The same two facts are asserted in `check_skew_correction` with their sign the right way round and their size printed, so the property is recorded rather than hidden by omission | `src/bin/validate.rs` | §74.10 |
| A 2:1-refined mesh EXCLUDED from the cuFFT direct Poisson path by name, with the exclusion measured — `pressure::cartesian::detect` is run on one and the sentence it prints is recorded, and the unrefined base grid of the same box is checked to still pass, so the refusal is about the refinement and not about the generator. No capacitance-matrix repair is offered: the trick §52.9 already refused needs O(1) modified rows and a refined block is O(N^(2/3)) of them | `src/pressure/cartesian.rs` | §74.8 |
| Bounding of `k` and `epsilon` | `src/turbulence.rs` | §6.1 |
| A driver may not default a MOMENTUM equation's convection entry to the `bounded` form. §3.1's correction subtracts `V_P (div u)_P` on the argument that `div u` is a convergence error; §25.1 makes it a prescribed constraint, so on a momentum equation it removes real converged momentum. Measured at -3.787% of the §32.5.2 force balance and closed to -0.000% by honouring the case's own entry — **and that measurement does not reproduce since §26.1**, which showed the dilatation it was integrating against to be itself an artefact of §25.1's `Q` being implemented without its conduction term (the same `bounded` run now closes the balance to +0.000%). The RULE stands: a strongly heated plume's dilatation is real and the correction still eats it. On §26's energy equation the correction is applied unconditionally and the flag is documented as not being a setting - but NOT because it is physics there, which §26.1 measured false on the mass flux; because dropping it leaves the CONSERVATIVE form, which for an ideal gas at fixed `p0` is identically `(gamma/(gamma-1)) p0 div(u)` and carries no information about `T` (measured: `Nu` 128.5 against 71.7) | `src/momentum.rs`, `src/energy.rs`, `src/bin/lowmach.rs` | §3.1, §13.4.1, §26.1, §32.5.5 |
| `NO_WALL = 1e10` m in a domain with no wall in it, rather than a singular all-Neumann Poisson solve — which is also the physically right answer, since `F_1 → 0` and the van Driest length grows without bound | `src/walldistance.rs` | §6.6 |
| Reading the wall faces from the mesh's `PatchKind::Wall` rather than from any one field's `boundaryField`, since the wall distance is a property of the geometry | `src/walldistance.rs` | §6.6 |
| Cell extents measured as `2·max_f \|Cf − C\|` over the cell's own faces — exact for an axis-aligned hexahedron, and biased *down* (so `nu_t` down) on a skewed one, because the mesh does not retain the points a cell was built from | `cuda/les.cu` | §16.2, §16.3 |
| `y+` for van Driest damping from the LOCAL wall-normal shear, with the wall normal taken from `grad y` — no nearest-wall search, and van Driest's own reading of `A+` | `cuda/les.cu`, `src/les.rs` | §16.4 |
| Applying van Driest damping only where `y+ > 0`, so that a quiescent field or a wall-free box keeps its geometric filter width instead of having it annihilated | `cuda/les.cu` | §16.4 |
| Filter-width smoothing: ratio 1.15, two sweeps, off by default, raising rather than lowering — the same sweep as §13.2's local time step | `src/les.rs`, `cuda/les.cu` | §16.5 |
| The unstructured-mesh test filter for Deardorff — a face-neighbour gather that reduces to FDS's `(1, 2, 1)/4` kernel in one dimension, because an unstructured mesh has no diagonal neighbours | `cuda/les.cu` | §6.5 |
| `C_s = 0.168`, Lilly's inertial-range value, rather than a wall-tuned fit — with the reasoning, and the dictionary entry that overrides it, in the code | `src/models/les.rs` | §6.5 |
| `RasModel::Les` as a variant of the model enum, so a RANS-only driver refuses an LES case by name instead of reading it as laminar | `src/models/registry.rs` | §13.4 |
| Residual normalisation | `src/solver.rs` | §8.4 |
| Density-ratio buoyancy `b = g(T_ref/T − 1)` | `src/momentum.rs` | §9 |
| The over-relaxed `0.05·\|d\|` floor on the delta coefficient | `src/mesh/geometry.rs` | §2.4 |
| The limited-snGrad expression `min(1, α·\|orth\|/(\|corr\|+ε))` | `src/io/schemes.rs` | §12.3 |
| The LTS smoothing sweep and damping | `src/timescheme.rs` | §13.2 |
| `-permissive` and the loud-failure contract | `src/io/contract.rs` | §13.4 |
| Each field owning its own wall-function decision | `src/wallfunctions.rs` | §15.5 |
| The `wallTreatment` preset table (`standard`/`spalding`/`rough`/`lowRe`) and its precedence (explicit per-field type > per-patch override > case default) — one setting expands to per-field `BcKind`s at case-build time; the per-face kernel architecture of §4 never sees a preset, only what it expands to | `src/io/case.rs`, `src/blockgen.rs`, `src/field_setup.rs` | §29.1 |
| The consistency contract: the four (five, with `T`) per-field types on one wall patch must belong to one preset row — a mixed row is a §13.4 error naming the patch, the offending pair and the consistent completions; `-permissive` substitutes the row implied by the `nut` choice and says so | `src/field_setup.rs`, `src/io/contract.rs` | §29.1, §13.4 |
| `validate_low_re_wall_treatment`: a `lowRe` wall treatment is only valid under a turbulence model that itself integrates through the viscous sublayer — `LaunderSharmaKE` (§33) is the one model on that menu today, `kEpsilon`/`kOmega`/`kOmegaSST` still are not, so resolving `lowRe` under any of the latter three is a §13.4 error naming the menu and the alternative (`standard`); `-permissive` substitutes `standard` and says so. SPEC-LIT §32's own second finding — `cases/channelPeriodicFluxLowRe.jsonc` still diverged after its under-resolved side walls were corrected, because standard k-epsilon has no near-wall damping at all, not because the mesh was wrong | `src/io/case.rs`, `src/io/case_json.rs` | §32, §13.4 |
| The `grad(grad U)` route for the `E` term — the Gauss gradient of the ALREADY-COMPUTED cell velocity gradient (`RasCore::update_flow_derived`'s own `grad_u`), rather than a dedicated second-derivative stencil, boundary faces zero-gradient-extrapolated because `grad U` carries no boundary field of its own; SPEC-LIT §33.1 marks this *DESIGN* itself and states its cost (one more CSR gather, roughly three times a vector-gradient pass, paid once per outer iteration) | `src/turbulence.rs` (`ls_grad_grad_u_mag_sqr`), `cuda/turbulence.cu` (`turbLsGradGradUMagSqr`) | §33.1 |
| `MeshResolutionReport` — the §33.2 mesh check (worst wall-adjacent y+, cells globally below y+ 20) as a pure function of already-downloaded `k`/wall-distance/wall-face-owner arrays rather than a GPU kernel, so a driver can call it once at set-up without a new device dependency; counted GLOBALLY rather than per wall-normal column, a stated approximation (this crate carries no wall-normal-column topology) | `src/models/launder_sharma.rs` (`mesh_resolution_report`) | §33.2 |
| Reusing [`u_plus`]'s own exponential (Kader) blend for the thermal law `T+`, rather than the root-sum-square blend `epsilon`/`omega` use — the log branch is stated in terms of `u+` itself (§29.3), so sharing the blend makes the `Pr = Pr_t` identity exact rather than approximate | `src/wallfunctions.rs` | §29.3 |
| The thermal wall function rewrites the Robin triple as the `fr = 0` (fixedGradient) degenerate case, with `ref_grad` chosen so the total flux is exactly the analytic Jayatilleke `q_w` whatever `k_eff` the energy equation computed that face — the same lagged-coefficient convention every other wall quantity in this crate already runs at | `src/wallfunctions.rs`, `src/energy.rs` | §29.3 |
| Kays-Crawford's `Pr_t(Pe_t)` evaluated in the algebraically rearranged form `Pr_t = Pr_t_inf/(1/2 + h(u))`, `h(u) = (e^-u + u - 1)/u^2`, `u = 1/(C Pe_t sqrt(Pr_t_inf))` - the same function as the literature form, with both limits reduced to one line each and the large-`Pe_t` cancellation removed (the literature form is 3.6% wrong at `Pe_t = 1e8`); one branch at each end, at `Pe_t -> 0` because a resolved wall face hands it `Pe_t = 0` exactly and the direct form is NaN there | `src/energy.rs`, `cuda/energy.cu` | §37.2 |
| §37's `Pr_t` reaches §26's `k_eff` and nothing else. §29.3's Jayatilleke wall function keeps `Pr_t_inf` BY DERIVATION - its `Pr_t(u+ + P)` is the log-layer value, which is exactly Kays-Crawford's own `Pe_t -> inf` limit, and `P` already carries its own sublayer integral - which is what makes a wall-function mesh a CONTROL for the §32 experiment; §17's `G_b` keeps the constant too, and `KaysCrawford` with gravity on is therefore a §13.4 error rather than a silent second `Pr_t` | `src/energy.rs`, `src/bin/lowmach.rs` | §37.3, §29.3, §17 |
| `PrtModel` defaults to `constant`, and `Prt` is not renamed - under `KaysCrawford` the SAME entry is read as `Pr_t_inf` and the sublayer value is `2 Prt` by derivation rather than a second entry a case could set inconsistently. The default is frozen so that every measurement recorded before §37 stays reproducible without editing a case file | `src/energy.rs`, `src/io/case_json.rs` | §37.4 |
| The `C_3` default for buoyancy production | `src/turbulence.rs` | §17 |
| The inert-species choice for the sum-to-one constraint | `src/species.rs` | §19 |
| Geometric cell-set selection for sources | `src/sources.rs` | §18 |
| Zalesak limiter iteration count | `src/vof.rs` | §20.2 |
| `-permissive`, and the one-line-per-setting warning it prints | `src/io/contract.rs` | §13.4 |
| The three sub-blocks of `output` are three PURPOSES, and each takes only the formats that serve it — `visualisation` is `vdb`/`nvdb`, `exact` is `vtu`/`openfoam`, `restart` is always `.mcr`. A format from the wrong column is a §13.4 error that says which column it belongs in, because "ofgpu does not write vtu" would be a lie | `src/io/output_plan.rs` | §44.1 |
| `output.visualisation.precision` is the ONE place in the case format where reduced precision is legitimate. `output.exact.precision` and `output.restart.precision` exist as fields purely so they can be refused with a REASON rather than as "unknown field" — a lossy "exact" format is a contradiction in the name, and §5.1's argument for carrying `phi` across a restart rules out a rounded flux as much as a re-derived one | `src/io/output_plan.rs`, `src/io/case_json.rs` | §44.3 |
| `restart.keep: 0` means "keep every checkpoint", not "keep none" — the safe reading of a zero is the one that deletes nothing, and it is also the only way a case can ask for an unpruned series | `src/restart.rs` | §44.5 |
| `restart::Checkpoints` deletes only paths in its own `written` list — files *this* value returned from *its own* `write`. A `restart_*.mcr` left by an earlier run, or put there by a person, is never a candidate, because it was never in the list. A directory scan with a glob would delete it; this cannot | `src/restart.rs` | §44.5 |
| A case that carries the `output` block AND a command line naming `-output`/`-writeInterval`/`-restartWrite` is a §13.4 error naming both, rather than a silent winner. `ofgpu-lowmach`'s `run.endTime` disclosure line is the other pattern and is right THERE because the case's `endTime` still reaches §31.3's contract; nothing in `output` reaches anything else | `src/io/output_plan.rs`, `src/bin/{lowmach,k_epsilon}.rs` | §44.6 |
| A positive `output.*.interval` on a STEADY run is refused by name: those runs advance an iteration counter, not a clock. `interval` absent (or `0`) means "the final state, once" and runs everywhere | `src/io/output_plan.rs` | §44.4 |
| A steady `ofgpu-lowmach` run given `-writeInterval` is refused by name rather than silently zeroed, and its own schedule is `-writeEvery N` in iterations - the same `next = t0 + W` schedule with the iteration count as the clock, so the schedule arithmetic never knows which it is driving | `src/bin/lowmach.rs`, `src/io/output_plan.rs` | §44.4 |
| A run ends in one of four named ways - budget, diverged, refused, error - with exit codes 0, 2, 3, 1 and one last stdout line saying which; a refusal is its own error variant so the code is chosen by type, never by reading the message | `src/error.rs`, `src/io/contract.rs`, `src/bin/lowmach.rs` | §31.4 |
| A steady `ofgpu-lowmach` run labels its final time directory with its iteration count and carries that count in the `.mcr` `time` slot, so `-iters N` after `-restartFrom` is "N more" and `0/` is never written: the driver used to label every write `format_time_name(t)` with a `t` that never moved on a steady run, and 3000 iterations landed on top of the initial fields. The convention is the one `io::case` already reads a steady `endTime` by | `src/bin/lowmach.rs` | §44.9 |
| The device-memory report `ofgpu-lowmach` prints is a DIFFERENCE from the free memory the driver saw before the first field existed, not `total - free`. On a desktop card `total - free` is dominated by the compositor and the browser, which is not a property of the run; the difference is, and it is the only form in which two configurations can be compared on the same machine. Sampled at three points (after setup, after the first step, after the last) rather than every step, because nothing in the time loop allocates after the first iteration and a `cuMemGetInfo` per step would put a driver round trip and a stream synchronise inside the wall time the same table quotes | `src/bin/lowmach.rs` (`MemWatch`) | §44 |
| `visualisation.fields` is checked TWICE — once before the time loop against the names the driver is about to build, and again inside `FieldSelection::apply` at every write. The early check is what stops a six-hour run failing at its first write; the late one is what makes the early list safe to trust | `src/io/output_plan.rs`, `src/bin/lowmach.rs` | §44.2 |
| `OutputPipeline` owns each stage's write COUNTER. The low-Mach driver passed a hard-coded `step: 0` and rebuilt its writers inside its write function, so every `.vtu`/`.vdb`/`.nvdb` of a run overwrote the last and the `.pvd`/`.usda` series never grew past one entry. A counter the pipeline increments, and writers built once, cannot be forgotten by the next driver | `src/io/output_plan.rs`, `src/bin/lowmach.rs` | §44.4 |
| The `.usda` scene's `filePath` extension follows the volume format actually selected instead of the hard-coded `"vdb"` — `-output nvdb,usda` used to write a scene pointing at files that do not exist | `src/io/output_plan.rs` | §44.1 |
| **Always** blending the two branches of the law of the wall — there is no `blended` switch, because there is no case in which the discontinuous form is wanted | `src/wallfunctions.rs`, `src/io/case.rs` | §6.4 |
| Evaluating the boundary conditions that read another field (`turbulentIntensity…`, `turbulentMixingLength…`, `totalPressure`, `pressureInletOutletVelocity`) once at setup, and refreshing only their value fraction from the flux thereafter | `src/field_setup.rs` | §4 |
| Defaulting `C_3` to the Henkes form, and spelling the override `C3Buoyancy` so it cannot collide with §6.1's dilatation `C_3` | `src/turbulence.rs`, `src/bin/buoyant.rs` | §17 |
| The `CoupledTurbulence` trait (`initialise`/`correct`/`nut`/`name`/`output_fields[_mut]`) - kept to exactly what a coupled driver's outer loop, writer seam and `.mcr` checkpoint need, and no more; a `dyn` dispatch is acceptable here (one virtual call per OUTER iteration) where it would not be in a standalone driver, because which model runs is a runtime fact read out of the case rather than known at the call site | `src/models/coupled.rs` | §30.2 |
| The LES wall-model preset mapping under `simulationType LES` - `standard`/`spalding` both read as `wernerWengleWallFunction`, `lowRe` as `nutLowReWallFunction` (`nu_t,w = 0`), and `rough` is a §13.4 error naming the two rather than an alias (a rough LES wall model is future work) | `src/io/case.rs` (`WallTreatment::les_nut_type`) | §30.1 |
| `ScalarBc::ThermalWallFunction { value }` - an explicit, per-patch `T_w` for `thermalWallFunction` on `T`, alongside the case-level `wallTreatment` auto-completion (which has no wall temperature of its own to write, only the neighbour cell's) - the same "explicit wall-function variant carries its own value" shape `TurbBc` already gives `nut`/`k`/`epsilon`/`omega` | `src/io/case_json.rs` | §29.1, §29.3 |
| The integrated wall-heat-flux report in `ofgpu-lowmach` - reading the SAME `(fr, ref_value, ref_grad)` Robin triple the energy matrix was assembled from, generically over every `wall`-kind patch, rather than re-deriving Jayatilleke or a plain Dirichlet flux externally per BC type | `src/bin/lowmach.rs` | §29.3, §30.3 |
| Keeping the STABLE branch of `G_b` in the `k` equation only, unless the case asks for it in `epsilon` as well | `cuda/turbulence.cu` | §17 |
| Selecting a source's cells geometrically — box, sphere, explicit list — and the `constant/fvSources` dictionary that expresses it | `src/sources.rs` | §18 |
| Refusing a source whose selection catches no cells | `src/sources.rs` | §18, §13.4 |
| Choosing the inert species by the largest volume-weighted mean when the case names none | `src/species.rs` | §19 |
| Writing `phi` at round-trip precision rather than at `writePrecision`, because its value carries a discrete constraint and not just a number | `src/io/fields.rs` | §5.1, §22 |
| Three iterations of the Zalesak limiter, and the GLOBAL bounds `[0, 1]` rather than the local-extremum ones | `cuda/vof.cu` | §20.2 |
| `eps = 1e-8/L` in the interface-normal normalisation, with `L` the cube root of the mean cell volume | `src/vof.rs` | §20.1 |
