| Mundo's splash criterion is decided in its FOURTH POWER. `K = Oh Re^1.25` is never formed; `K^4 = We^2 Re` is, because `Oh Re = sqrt(We)` exactly and `x -> x^4` is strictly increasing on the non-negatives. The same decision, with no `pow` and no `sqrt` — which matters because §38.6 records that `pow` with a non-integer exponent is not bit-stable across compute capabilities, and a regime map that flips on the hardware is a different model on the hardware. Bai & Gosman's `La^(-0.18)` cannot be written this way, and that asymmetry is why `mundo` is the default | `cuda/parcels.cu` (`ofpImpactRegime`), `src/parcels/impact.rs` | §78.4, §38.6 |
| The design note's splash parameter is not Mundo's. The note gives `K = Oh^(-1/2) We^(3/4)` against `K_crit ~ 57.7`; Mundo's is `K = Oh Re^1.25`, and substituting `Oh = sqrt(We)/Re` shows the two differ by `Re^(1/4)` — a factor of 5.5 at the `Re ~ 900` of a 100 um water droplet at 9 m/s, where the note's expression returns 316 against its own stated threshold of 57.7. The implementation follows the paper | `src/parcels/impact.rs` | §78.13 |
| Splash is DETECTED and not enacted, and the consequence is published rather than hidden: the parent deposits whole, `n_splash` is a first-class counter, and the wall deposit is stated as an upper bound by that many impacts. Bai & Gosman's own retained-mass fraction is declined for two reasons — it is stochastic and this crate has no RNG (§66.8), and deleting the un-retained part would break the exact partition the section exists for | `cuda/parcels.cu`, `src/parcels.rs` (`ParcelStats::n_splash`) | §78.7 |
| A rule that condemned every `§NN.M` in a source comment that is not in `SPEC-LIT.md` would have "fixed" **171 correct citations** — Patankar §4.2, Jasak §3.4.3, Saad §6.7/§12.4, Ferziger & Perić §7.5, `docs/07-lowmach-solver.md` §1.1. §80's audit therefore attributes a citation before it checks it: it belongs to the last document named on its own line or the line before, and to `SPEC-LIT.md` otherwise. The window width was measured, not chosen — one line excuses 135 and flags 47, two lines excuse 203 and flag 19, the whole comment block excuses 307 and starts swallowing real SPEC-LIT citations under a module header that named ASHRAE once |
| `cuda/fv.cu` cited `(S3.3)` and `(S3.5)` for Jasak's Green-Gauss gradient and TVD ratio. **Both resolve against `SPEC-LIT.md`** — as §3.3 "Temporal" and §3.5 "Gradients" — so an existence check passes them for ever while they point at the wrong document. Only naming the work on the citation's own line makes them right, and only reading the bibliography entry made them visible: this is the class of defect §80.8 states it cannot automate away |
| The §77 recurrence is a spelling problem, and it is now counted: **252 symbols in `SPEC-LIT.md` are BOTH an equation label and a subsection number**, and **972 source citations use the bare `SNN.M` form on one of them**, which does not say which is meant. That is how one token for 77.5 came to mean §77.4 in one file, §77.6 in another and §77.5 in a third. §80.4 ratchets the count: it may fall, it may not rise, and new code writes `§NN.M` or `(NN.M)` — or, in an ASCII-only file, `(SPEC-LIT NN.M)` or `(SNN.M)` — each of which resolves to exactly one thing |
| Two semantic rules were measured against the tree and REJECTED rather than shipped weakened: requiring a possessive gloss (``§NN.M's `word` ``) to occur in that subsection's own body matches only **81.1 %** of 872 sites, and requiring a widely-cited symbol to quote a distinctive word of its own heading matches **78.4 %** of 241 — the misses are headings like "Validation" and "Discretisation" that have no distinctive word. A gate with a 19 % false-positive rate is a gate that gets deleted |
| A fenced line ending in `(NN.M)` is not necessarily a label. §80's parser applied "a reference does not define the symbol it cites" to prose only, so §77's validation row `cooling / expansion   17.59   (17.6)` — a MEASURED VALUE beside a computed one — defined an equation `(17.6)` for §17, a section with no subsections and no equations of any kind. `S17.6` would have resolved against a symbol that does not exist, and §17 entered the set of sections that label equations, making §80.2's own sentence ("twenty-nine, all §40 or later") true of the document and false of the code enforcing it, which counted thirty. A label now defines only inside a fenced block OF ITS OWN SECTION: 302 labels become 301, thirty sections become twenty-nine, and the sentence is true of both |
| The distinction between a `work` (excused) and a `document` (checked) held only while the path did. Both took the same branch when the file could not be read, so hiding `docs/07-lowmach-solver.md` turned a deliberately broken citation into it from a failure into a SILENT PASS — one `git mv` from a promise that had stopped being kept with nothing said. A missing `document` now fails its citations, and a test fails first and names the file |
| An ambiguous form is worth removing before it is worth counting. §80.4 ratcheted the bare `SNN.M` and left `(SNN.M)` — 357 citations — bounded by nothing, so the ledger's stated purpose ("the next file to add one fails the build") was false for a quarter of the population. Resolving `(SNN.M)` exactly like `(NN.M)` costs EIGHT corrections in the whole tree, every one a subsection written in the parenthesised spelling and one of them inside a user-facing refusal message. The same tightening applied to the bare form fails 586 sites, which is why that one is ratcheted and this one is not. The replacement spelling is form B, `(SPEC-LIT 78.7)`, because all five files are ASCII-only — no `.cu` file in this tree contains a single `§` |
| A rule loose in one direction manufactures false findings as readily as it excuses true ones, and §80 printed one of each. Its own "at least one real dangling reference inside `SPEC-LIT.md`" was `(S36.1)` in §62.6, argued from §36 labelling no equations. §36 labels none — which is exactly the case where the parenthesised form resolves against HEADINGS, and §36.1 is a heading. The citation was correct all along |
| G5's reference length: `A_max(c)` is the largest PLANAR FACE GROUP of a cell — the faces whose OUTWARD unit normals agree to within 5 degrees, their `\|Sf\|` summed — and not the largest single face, so that the four coplanar quarter-faces §74's 2:1 interface gives a coarse cell read as the one face they geometrically are. A per-face maximum let the ammonia case's own 13 m x 13 m x 5 cm sliver through the gate at a refinement interface (`tau` 0.0115 whole, 0.0923 split — a factor of `4^(3/2)`), which is the defect the gate exists to refuse. The 5-degree tolerance and the outward orientation are ours; the grouping errs conservatively (a larger `A_max` is a smaller `tau`), so it can cost a refusal and never a silent pass | `src/automesher/quality.rs` | §92.3 |
| G6's conditioning limit `cond(T_c) < 1e4` — four decades of the six an f64 residual can afford to lose. Recorded with what it does NOT do: on a box cell `tau_c cond(T_c) = 3 sqrt(a/b)`, so on a slab G5's `0.05` is already `cond = 60` and G6 is 167 times looser; G6 earns its place only on the near-coplanar shapes G5 cannot see | `src/automesher/quality.rs` | §92.3 |
| The 2015 gamma model's production is Kato–Launder `nu_t S Omega` in BOTH the `k` and the `omega` equation, stamped over SST's own production wherever the model is attached, with SST-2003's `k`-production limiter re-applied to the Kato–Launder `G`. The TMR page writes ONE `P_k`, reads it in both equations and notes it is not SST's; the alternative reading — Kato–Launder in `k` only, the standard form in `omega` — needs the page to write two productions, and it writes one. The consequence is stated in the spec and measured in Gate 90-R: at `gamma = 1` this model is SST with Kato–Launder production, not plain SST | `src/models/k_omega_sst.rs`, `cuda/gmtrans.cu` | §90.6 |
| The Mach number a low-Mach run prints is a HOST reduction over the fields the driver has already downloaded for its iteration line - no kernel, no capture-registry entry - and the run refuses above `M = 0.3` because at that Mach the isentropic density ratio is already `rho/rho0 = (1 + 0.2 M^2)^-2.5 = 0.956`, a 4.4 % density change (`gamma = 1.4`), which is where "Mach much less than 1" stops being a description of the run | `src/energy.rs` (`mach_number`, `refuse_above_low_mach`), `src/bin/lowmach.rs` | §93.5, §93.6 |
| A partition may not cut a `PatchKind::Interface` face. §47.2 consequence 2 makes conservation across the interface a property of ONE kernel writing both triples from ONE pair list; two parts would recompute `h_G` from two copies and could differ in the last bit. Refused at `Decomposition::from_map`, by name, with the first offending face | `src/decompose.rs` | §93.6 |
| Gate 93-A compares the PRE-FOLD `diag/upper/lower/source` (and the boundary coefficients of non-interface faces) of a region's rows against the region built alone, bit for bit, and deliberately does NOT compare the solved field - §47.2's last paragraph: the two are different-sized linear systems whose Krylov iterates are different numbers | `src/cht.rs` (`region_rows`), `src/cht/tests.rs`, `src/bin/validate.rs` | §93.8 |
| The pressure force on a wall is `sum_f rho_f p_f Sf_f` with `p` the KINEMATIC pressure this crate carries and `rho_f` the wall density, `p_f` the face's evaluated boundary value (first order on `zeroGradient`); the viscous force vector is §32.5.1's own per-face traction given its direction, never a second gradient. When a run has no streamwise axis the force report projects each patch on ITS OWN mean traction direction and says `DEFAULT`, rather than assuming `x`. The flat-plate estimate's `5e5` transition is a textbook number, printed with its regime | `src/wallfunctions.rs`, `src/bin/lowmach.rs` | §32.5.6 |
| Each Krylov scalar update (`beta`, `alpha`, `omega`, the `rho` roll, the convergence test) is done by thread 0 of the stage-two reduction that produces its operand, in the same launch, with the same loads and IEEE operations in the same order, so the fused PBiCGStab and PCG loops are the unfused ones bit for bit - held to a verbatim copy of the old loops. A solve that would make a host round-trip inside a CUDA-graph capture is refused by the solve, by name. `checkInterval` is read strictly from `fvSolution` and defaults to a named constant | `cuda/solver.cu`, `src/solver.rs`, `src/io/case.rs` | §113 |

## GPU plumbing and tooling — original

No external source. These have no counterpart in any CFD code we are aware of,
and they are a larger share of the value than their line counts suggest.

| File | What it is |
|---|---|
| `src/device.rs` | The cudarc wrapper: context, dedicated non-blocking stream, `DevBuf`, `KernelSet`, CUDA graph capture, and the per-process pool reading and allocation trace of SPEC-LIT §111.2 (`cuDeviceGetMemPool`, `cuMemPoolGetAttribute`; documented driver API) |
| `src/error.rs` | The error type |
| `src/types.rs` | `Vec3` / `Tensor`, `#[repr(C)]` mirrors of the device structs, with a layout test |
| `build.rs` | The MSVC/nvcc build glue: `vcvars64.bat` capture, CUBIN emission, `/Zc:preprocessor` |
| `src/mesh.rs`, `src/mesh/topology.rs` | The mesh types, the LDU→CSR inversion, and the merged GLOBAL-face-ordered row map (§70) |
| `src/ldu.rs` | The matrix type and the LDU→CSR permutation for AMGX/cuDSS |
| `src/field.rs` | The device field types and `BcKind` |
| `src/pressure/mod.rs` | The `PressureBackend` trait and the measuring selector — hard filter, then accuracy check, then measured timing |
| `src/pressure/fft.rs`, `cuda/pressure.cu` | The cuFFT direct Poisson solver. Method: Swarztrauber, *SIAM Review* 19 (1977) 490; Press et al., *Numerical Recipes* §19.4 |
| `src/pressure/cartesian.rs` | Detecting whether a mesh is a uniform Cartesian block, which is what makes the FFT path applicable — and, §74.8, what EXCLUDES a mesh with 2:1 refinement interfaces from the FFT path, measured by running the detector on one and recording the sentence it prints rather than by asserting it |
| `src/pressure/amgx.rs` | The AMGX backend. AMGX itself is BSD-3-Clause; see `../NOTICE` |
| `src/blockgen.rs` | The structured mesh generator |
| `src/mesh/refined.rs` | The STATIC 2:1-refined hexahedral mesh generator (§74.2): a box born with coarse-fine interfaces, so the operators of §3 can be measured on one. **ORIGINAL** — it emits a plain polyMesh and the geometry sweep of §2 does the rest; nothing here adapts a mesh. Written from **Berger & Colella, *J. Comput. Phys.* 82(1) (1989) 64** (DOI 10.1016/0021-9991(89)90035-1) for what a 2:1 interface IS, and **cited to be distinguished from** — §74.1 argues a face-based cell-centred code needs no flux register and says why; **Isaac, Burstedde & Ghattas, *IPDPS 2012* 426** (DOI 10.1109/IPDPS.2012.47) for the 2:1 balance condition, whose O(1)-ripple algorithm is **named and NOT implemented** (the fixed-point sweep is used instead, and the reason is stated); **Muzaferija & Gosman, *J. Comput. Phys.* 138(2) (1997) 766** (DOI 10.1006/jcph.1997.5853) for locally refined FV on arbitrary polyhedra. All four DOIs in §74 checked against Crossref on 2026-09-01 by fetching `api.crossref.org/works/<doi>`. **p4est is GPL-2.0-or-later, libsc LGPL-2.1 and t8code GPL-2.0 — the licences were checked BEFORE anything was opened and none of the three was opened**, contrary to the brief's assumption that p4est was BSD-2; the papers are read, the code is not. OpenFOAM's `dynamicRefineFvMesh` (GPL-3.0) was not opened either |
| `src/adapt.rs`, `cuda/adapt.cu` | The adapt (§75): the linear-octree `Forest`, the emitter, the refinement criteria, the marking with hysteresis, the 2:1 balance fixed point and the plan. The error indicator is **Löhner, *Comput. Methods Appl. Mech. Engrg.* 61(3) (1987) 323** (DOI 10.1016/0045-7825(87)90098-3), whose ratio is his and whose surface-integral finite-volume form is **DESIGN** — Löhner wrote it for nodal shape functions. The `D*/dx` resolution measure is the **FDS User's Guide, NIST SP 1019, "Mesh Resolution"** — a **US Government work, public domain**, present locally at `reference/fds/Manuals/`. The 2:1 balance condition is **Isaac, Burstedde & Ghattas, *IPDPS 2012* 426** (DOI 10.1109/IPDPS.2012.47), whose O(1)-ripple algorithm is **named and NOT implemented**; the leaf-set-only representation and the sort-then-search shape are **Sundar, Sampath & Biros, *SIAM J. Sci. Comput.* 30(5) (2008) 2675** (DOI 10.1137/070681727). **Jasak & Gosman, *Numer. Heat Transfer B* 38(3) (2000) 237 and 257** (DOI 10.1080/10407790050192753, 10.1080/10407790050192762) are the principled estimator and its driver, **named and NOT implemented** and refused by name in §75.9. **ORIGINAL**: the canonical leaf ordering that makes the emitter bit-for-bit identical to §74.2's static generator, the no-free-list rebuild-by-re-sort, and the promotion/cancellation fixed point. Every DOI checked against Crossref on 2026-09-01 by fetching `api.crossref.org/works/<doi>`. **p4est (GPL-2.0-or-later), libsc (LGPL-2.1), t8code (GPL-2.0) and OpenFOAM's `dynamicRefineFvMesh` (GPL-3.0) were not opened** |
| `src/adapt/rebuild.rs` | The addressing rebuild (§75.5): the LDU face order and the cell → face CSR, from one stable sort and two binary searches. **ORIGINAL**, and it is a **correction to the design note this section was written against**: the note specified "two binary searches plus an exclusive scan", and there is no scan — a `lower_bound` over a sorted array already IS the exclusive prefix sum of the per-cell counts, so `cf_offset[c] = lower_bound(owner,c) + lower_bound(nbrKey,c)` exactly, and it is asserted cell by cell. The note also described the within-cell order as "owned faces then neighboured faces", which is **not** what `mesh::topology::build_cell_face_maps` produces; the interleaved ascending order is reproduced by a two-pointer merge, one thread per cell, no atomic. The radix sort is **Knuth, *TAOCP* vol. 3, 2nd ed. (1998) §5.2.5** — a book — and is written to compute what `cub::DeviceRadixSort` computes so that a fully device-resident adapt can substitute the library primitive. No external code of any licence was read |
| `src/adapt/transfer.rs` | The conservative transfer (§75.6): restriction, prolongation and the identity as one pair of gathers. The reconstruction limiter is **Barth & Jespersen, AIAA 89-0366, *27th Aerospace Sciences Meeting* (1989)** (DOI 10.2514/6.1989-366). **Berger & Colella, *J. Comput. Phys.* 82(1) (1989) 64** (DOI 10.1016/0021-9991(89)90035-1) is the restriction/prolongation pair, and its flux-register apparatus stays **cited to be distinguished from** (§74.1). **Balsara, *J. Comput. Phys.* 174(2) (2001) 614** (DOI 10.1006/jcph.2001.6917) is the divergence-free flux prolongation, **named and NOT implemented** — the face flux is not transferred at all and §75.11 says so. **ORIGINAL**, and a **correction to the design note**: the note's multiplicative conservative rescale `lambda = rho phi V / sum rho phihat V` is **singular for any field with zero volume-weighted mean over a parent**, and recentring the reconstruction on the weight-weighted centroid of the children makes the conserved sum telescope exactly with no rescale at all. Measured both ways. DOIs checked against Crossref on 2026-09-01 |
| `src/decompose.rs` | The domain decomposition (§71): the partition map, the ghost layer, the local renumbering and the exchange plan. The Hilbert index is written from **Skilling, *AIP Conf. Proc.* 707 (2004) 381** (DOI 10.1063/1.1751381) and **Butz, *IEEE Trans. Computers* C-20(4) (1971) 424** (DOI 10.1109/T-C.1971.223258) — papers, which `LICENSING.md` permits; the SFC-as-partitioner argument is **Aluru & Sevilgen, *HiPC* (1997) 230** (DOI 10.1109/HIPC.1997.634498) and the communication-volume metric is **Catalyurek & Aykanat, *IEEE TPDS* 10(7) (1999) 673** (DOI 10.1109/71.780863). **METIS 5.2.x is Apache-2.0** (`LICENSE` at `github.com/KarypisLab/METIS`, verified 2026-08-31) and **ParMETIS is proprietary/non-commercial** (`LICENSE` at `github.com/KarypisLab/ParMETIS`, verified 2026-08-31); neither is linked and neither source was read — §71.2 gives the reasons. The zero-halo-layer style, in which a cut face becomes an ordinary coupled boundary face, is described in **Weller, Tabor, Jasak & Fureby, *Computers in Physics* 12(6) (1998) 620** (DOI 10.1063/1.168744), a paper; OpenFOAM's source was not opened. §93.6 adds one refusal: a partition that puts the two cells of a `PatchKind::Interface` face on different parts is refused by name, because SPEC-LIT §47.2 consequence 2 requires ONE launch over ONE interface-pair list writing both sides |
| `src/exactsum.rs`, `src/exactsum/tests.rs`, `cuda/exactsum.cu` | The order-free accumulator (§72): reductions whose answer is a function of the multiset of terms, so that a cut cannot move one. **Derived** from the binned / indexed floating-point literature - **Demmel & Nguyen, *ARITH-21* (2013) 163** (DOI 10.1109/ARITH.2013.9) and ***IEEE Trans. Computers* 64(7) (2015) 2060** (DOI 10.1109/TC.2014.2345391); **Ahrens, Demmel & Nguyen, *ACM TOMS* 46(3) (2020) 1** (DOI 10.1145/3389360); **Collange, Defour, Graillat & Iakymchuk, *Parallel Computing* 49 (2015) 83** (DOI 10.1016/j.parco.2015.09.001); **Kulisch, *Computer Arithmetic and Validity*, 2nd ed., De Gruyter (2013)** (DOI 10.1515/9783110301793) - all **papers and a book**, every DOI checked against Crossref on 2026-08-31. **ExBLAS and ReproBLAS are BSD-3-Clause and would have been readable; neither was opened**, and the limb width `W = 30`, the limb count `K = 4`, the single GLOBAL anchor and the gather-not-reduce collective are ORIGINAL choices argued in §72.2-§72.4. **Iakymchuk et al., *J. Comput. Appl. Math.* 371 (2020) 112697** (DOI 10.1016/j.cam.2019.112697) is the closest published work and is cited as such. **Balaji & Kimpe, *HPCC/EUC* (2013) 407** (DOI 10.1109/HPCC.and.EUC.2013.65) is why no `MPI_Allreduce` or `ncclAllReduce` appears anywhere in the design; NVIDIA's NCCL environment-variable documentation, which promises no reduction order, is the other half of that argument. No GPL source was opened |
| `src/distsolve.rs`, `src/distsolve/tests.rs` | PCG and PBiCGStab over a decomposed mesh (§73). **ORIGINAL**: every kernel launched is one `cuda/solver.cu` or `cuda/precon.cu` already owns, unchanged, so the arithmetic is the serial solver's; what is new is where the exchange goes and which reduction is used. The recurrences are **Hestenes & Stiefel, *J. Res. NBS* 49(6) (1952) 409** (DOI 10.6028/jres.049.044, a **US Government work, public domain**) and **van der Vorst, *SIAM J. Sci. Stat. Comput.* 13(2) (1992) 631** (DOI 10.1137/0913035), as §8 already cites them; **Saad, *Iterative Methods for Sparse Linear Systems*, 2nd ed., SIAM (2003)** (DOI 10.1137/1.9780898718003) §6.7, §7.4.2, §12.4 and **ch. 14**, which is where block Jacobi is defined and is what the block-local factorisation is named as. **Cai & Sarkis, *SIAM J. Sci. Comput.* 21(2) (1999) 792** (DOI 10.1137/S106482759732678X) is cited **to be distinguished from, not followed**. **Ghysels & Vanroose, *Parallel Computing* 40(7) (2014) 224** (DOI 10.1016/j.parco.2013.06.001) and **Cools & Vanroose, *Parallel Computing* 65 (2017) 1** (DOI 10.1016/j.parco.2017.04.005) are the pipelined variants, **refused by name in §73.7** on the strength of **Cools, *Parallel Computing* 86 (2019) 16** (DOI 10.1016/j.parco.2019.05.002), which documents their accuracy loss. **Amdahl, *AFIPS '67 (Spring)* 483** (DOI 10.1145/1465482.1465560) is the shape of §73.6's scaling statement and of the one it declines to make. Every DOI here checked against Crossref on 2026-09-01 by fetching `api.crossref.org/works/<doi>`. All **papers and a book**; no solver source of any licence was opened, and no GPL source was consulted |
| `src/xref.rs` | The citation audit (§80): the Markdown heading/equation-label parser, the prose lexer that separates comments and string literals from code, the two-line attribution window, and the tests that hold every `§NN.M`, `SNN.M` and `(NN.M)` in the tree to something that exists. **ORIGINAL** and test-only (`#[cfg(test)] mod xref;` in `lib.rs`), so the release build is unchanged by construction. Its only inputs are `SPEC-LIT.md`'s own structure and the tree on disk; no external source of any kind was consulted, and the escape-hatch registry — Patankar, Jasak, Moukalled, Saad, Perić, Incropera, Modest, Numerical Recipes, and the two `docs/` documents — is parsed out of §80.3 rather than written here |
| `src/mesh/gpugeom.rs`, `src/mesh/gpugeom/tests.rs`, `cuda/meshgeom.cu` | The finite-volume geometry sweep on the device (§82). **ORIGINAL** — a line-for-line device restatement of this crate's own `src/mesh/geometry.rs`, whose physics and whose citations are §2's and are unchanged: **Jasak (1996) §3.2, §3.3.1, §3.4.2**, **Moukalled, Mangani & Darwish (2016) §6.4, §8.6.4**, **Ferziger & Perić (2002) §8.6**. Nothing is re-derived here; the section's own claim is about arithmetic reproducibility, for which the sources are **Goldberg, *ACM Computing Surveys* 23(1) (1991) 5** (DOI 10.1145/103162.103163) for the non-associativity of floating-point addition that makes the gather order a specification, and the ***NVIDIA CUDA C++ Programming Guide*** plus the `nvcc` reference for `-fmad`, read from NVIDIA's own documentation. **ORIGINAL to §82**: the reformulation of the host sweep's two per-cell SCATTERS as one gather over the cell → face CSR in the order that makes them bitwise; the per-unit `-fmad=false` and the second, CONTRACTED cubin that measures what the flag buys rather than asserting it; and the measurement of §82.2, which finds that §75.8 named the wrong half of the rebuild as its binding constraint. **OpenFOAM's `primitiveMesh`/`surfaceInterpolation` (GPL-3.0) and SU2 (LGPL) are the obvious references for a finite-volume geometry sweep and neither was opened** |
| `GpuMesh::from_device_geometry` in `src/mesh.rs`, `gpu_geometry_resident` and `GpuGeometry::download_volumes` in `src/mesh/gpugeom.rs`, `Forest::build_resident` and `adapt::plan_resident` in `src/adapt.rs` | The device-resident mesh rebuild (§83): an adapt whose geometry is computed on the device and stays there. **ORIGINAL, and no new external source was consulted for it.** The arithmetic is unchanged - every kernel is §82's, every host line is the one that was already there - so the physics citations remain §2's and §82's (**Jasak 1996**, **Moukalled, Mangani & Darwish 2016**, **Ferziger & Peric 2002**) and the reproducibility citation remains **Goldberg, *ACM Computing Surveys* 23(1) (1991) 5** (DOI 10.1145/103162.103163), which is why the one array that still returns returns rather than being reduced on the device: `total_volume` and §75.6's conservative weight are folds in ascending cell id, and a tree reduction is a different multiset order. **ORIGINAL to §83**: the single `GpuTopology::upload` that makes §70.3's row-map rebuild one rule reached two ways; the split of the §82 drop-in into a resident sweep plus a download, so that the two routes cannot run different host prologues; and the measurement of §83.3, which prices all three routes to the same place and finds a second sixteen-array trip - `GpuMesh::upload` - that §82.5 had measured and deliberately left out of its table. No solver source of any licence was opened |
| `cuda/meshemit.cu`, `src/mesh/gpuemit.rs`, `src/mesh/gpuemit/tests.rs`, the section 84 tests in `src/adapt.rs`, `Forest::emit_on_device`, `Forest::build_resident_on_device` and `adapt::plan_resident_on_device` in `src/adapt.rs`, `geometry::validate_csr` in `src/mesh/geometry.rs`, `gpu_geometry_resident_csr` in `src/mesh/gpugeom.rs` | The polyMesh EMITTER on the device (§84) — the voxel ownership map, the face grouping, the point numbering, the internal face list in (owner, neighbour) order and the boundary faces patch by patch. **ORIGINAL, and no external source was consulted for it.** It is a device restatement of this crate's own `adapt::Forest::emit`, which is itself §75's, and it introduces no numerics at all: the only floating-point in the translation unit is a grid index times a spacing, so the physics citations remain §2's and §74's and there is nothing new to attribute. **ORIGINAL to §84**: the **touch rank** (84.1) — the observation that the host's sequential first-touch point numbering is a total order on `(cell, axis, slot, corner)` and therefore a number, so the numbering becomes a minimum per grid point and one exclusive scan, both pure functions of the leaf set; the proof that the minimum can be **gathered** rather than reached with an `atomicMin`, because every rectangle this emitter emits is a whole face of some leaf and every lattice point on a leaf's closed box has an incident voxel inside it; the observation that `(owner, neighbour)` keys are unique, which turns the host's sort into a per-cell gather with no sort at all; and the patch-major boundary flag array, which makes one scan produce both the patch starts and the within-patch order. The exclusive scan is **`cuda/parcelsort.cu`'s** (§67.2), reused rather than copied. **OpenFOAM's `polyTopoChange`/`hexRef8` and p4est (GPL-2.0-or-later) are the obvious references for an adaptive mesh emitter and neither was opened**; nor was SU2 (LGPL) |
| `src/mesh/ale.rs`, `src/mesh/ale/tests.rs`, `cuda/ale.cu` | The moving mesh on the device (§105): resident points and face CSR, the in-place geometry recompute, the swept volume of a face, the scheme-weighted mesh flux, the volume history and the ALE time derivative. **ORIGINAL.** The conservation law it enforces is **Demirdžić & Perić (1988)**, DOI 10.1002/fld.1650080906 (the space conservation law) and **Thomas & Lombard (1979)**, DOI 10.2514/3.61273 (the geometric conservation law), cited as `docs/09` cites them; the geometry is §2's and §82's kernels, unchanged. **ORIGINAL to §105**: the swept volume as the exact integral over a linearly moving fan triangle, evaluated by Simpson's rule, which is exact for the quadratic its normal is; the identity that a quadrilateral's centroid offset is normal to its `Sf`, which makes §2.2's pyramid volume and the fan volume agree on every face of at most four vertices, warped or not; the mesh flux weighted by the time scheme's own coefficients; and the shared-borrow in-place write of §105.2. **OpenFOAM's `sweptVol`/`meshPhi`/`movePoints` (GPL-3.0) are the obvious references and none was opened; nor was SU2 (LGPL)** |
| `src/mesh/motion.rs`, `src/mesh/motion/tests.rs`, `src/ale_flow.rs`, `src/ale_flow/tests.rs` | The SIMPLE loop on a moving mesh (§105.7-§105.10): the relative flux routed through every convective consumer, the moving-wall value written on the device (the two kernels appended to `cuda/ale.cu` and their launchers in `src/mesh/ale.rs`), the prescribed-motion rules and the inverse-distance point smoother, and Gate 105-B. **ORIGINAL**, except the smoother's two-term weight, which is **Luke, Collins & Blades (2012)**, DOI 10.1016/j.jcp.2011.09.021, with this tree's own parameters. **ORIGINAL to §105**: which time derivative goes with which convective form on a moving mesh; the absolute flux kept in the pressure equation because the space conservation law closes the volume balance; the normal-only moving-wall value from the mesh flux; the stroking outlet as the time-order gate, and the reason an accelerating piston is not. **OpenFOAM's `dynamicMotionSolverFvMesh`, `fvc::makeRelative`, `movingWallVelocity` and its displacement motion solvers (GPL-3.0) are the obvious references and none was opened; nor was SU2 (LGPL)** |
| `src/turek_hron.rs`, `src/turek_hron/tests.rs` | §105.13, §105.14, §105.15 | Turek-Hron CFD1-CFD3 on the card and Gate 105-C. The benchmark - geometry, fluid, inflow profile and ramp, the forces on cylinder and flag and their periodic reduction - is **S. Turek and J. Hron, LNCSE 53 (2006) 371-385**, DOI 10.1007/3-540-34596-5_15, with its numbers read from the authors' open FEATFLOW benchmark page. **ORIGINAL**: the analytic wake wobble with every wall fixed, the parabola-refined per-period reduction, the fork of one spun-up state into a static and a wobbling continuation. The forces are §32.5.6's two integrators, called and not repeated. **OpenFOAM's `forces`/`forceCoeffs` function objects, its cylinder tutorials and `dynamicMotionSolverFvMesh` (GPL-3.0) were not opened; nor was SU2 (LGPL)** |
| `src/capture.rs`, `src/capture/registry.rs`, `src/capture/gates.rs` | The CUDA-graph capture gate (§81): the capture/replay/compare protocol, the node-census check, the source-derived registry that classifies every module which reaches the device, and the gates themselves. **ORIGINAL** and test-only (`#[cfg(test)] mod capture;` in `lib.rs`), so the release build is unchanged by construction. Its inputs are this crate's own modules and the tree on disk; the CUDA Graph semantics it relies on — `cuStreamBeginCapture`, `cuGraphGetNodes`, `cuGraphNodeGetType`, and the fact that `cuMemAllocAsync` is stream-ordered and therefore capturable — are the CUDA Driver API's published interface, read from NVIDIA's documentation. No external source code of any kind was consulted |
| `src/halo.rs`, `cuda/halo.cu` | The ghost-cell exchange (§71.4): the pack-as-gather kernel and the contiguous-receive layout that removes the unpack entirely. Original; no external source |
| `src/bin/decompose.rs` | `ofgpu-decompose`, the driver that cuts a shipped case into parts, runs it in one process and reports whether any bit moved (§71.9), then reduces over every relabelling of that cut and reports whether the reduction moved (§72.9), then runs a real PCG and PBiCGStab over the cut and over every relabelling of it and reports whether the field or the stopping iteration moved, with the block-local DIC/DILU iteration ladder and the per-iteration cost (§73.9) |
| `src/bin/*` | Drivers and benchmarks |
| `src/bin/vof.rs` | The two-phase driver and its surge-front report |
| `src/bin/common/mod.rs` | The drivers' shared case-reading seam, `CaseNumerics` included — see the *DESIGN* table above for the §13.4.1 row |
| `src/bin/sample.rs` | `ofgpu-sample`, the published fluid gates' post-processor (SPEC-LIT §110): the column and wall-patch sampler the §110.5 recipes measure with, four pure functions and their tests. Original; no external source |
| `src/bin/probe.rs`, `cuda/probe.cu` | The toolchain probe: nvcc → CUBIN → cudarc → launch → read-back, reproducing the CPU answer bit for bit |
| `src/device.rs` (§81.3, §81.4) | The capture guard and the graph node census, added on top of the existing cudarc wrapper: an `AtomicBool` that makes every host round-trip on `Gpu` refuse itself **by name** while a capture is recording, and `Graph::shape()`, which walks the instantiated graph with `cuGraphGetNodes`/`cuGraphNodeGetType` so that an allocation node inside an iteration is visible — stream-ordered allocation is *capturable*, so that defect otherwise succeeds silently. Original; the two driver entry points are documented API |
| `cuda/ofgpu_device.cuh` | The only header the kernels share: `ofscalar`, `ofvec3`, `oftensor` and the device-side helpers. Its layouts are mirrored by `src/types.rs`, and that pairing is pinned by a layout test |
| `src/lib.rs` | The crate root: module declarations, the crate-wide `Scalar` alias, the re-exports. No numerics of its own |
| `../tools/*.py` | The isometric volume renderer and the HTML report builder |

## The mesher — literature and original

`ofgpu-automesher` (SPEC-LIT §92) is this project's own mesher. **No GPL mesher
source was opened**: OpenFOAM's `snappyHexMesh` (GPL-3.0), cfMesh (GPL-3.0),
TetGen (AGPL-3.0) and CGAL's GPL modules were all identified as GPL before
anything was read, and none of them was read. The octree families §92.2's
stage 1 stands next to are named here for the same reason §74 and §75 name
them: **p4est (GPL-2.0-or-later), libsc (LGPL-2.1) and t8code (GPL-2.0)** were
identified as GPL/LGPL before anything was read and none was read — the 2:1
balance §92.2 reuses is `mesh::refined::balance_2to1`, this repository's own
(§74.2), and Isaac, Burstedde & Ghattas (2012) is cited as a PAPER.

**The documentation, named exactly.** "The OpenFOAM User Guide" is not one
document, and §92 leans on it for four facts, so both documents that name is
used for are recorded: CFD Direct's *OpenFOAM v12 User Guide* §5.5 "Mesh
generation with snappyHexMesh" (`doc.cfd.direct/openfoam/user-guide-v12/
snappyhexmesh`, © 2015-2025 CFD Direct Ltd, website documentation under CFD
Direct's terms of use, no Creative Commons grant stated), and ESI-OpenCFD's
*OpenFOAM User Guide* meshing chapter (`openfoam.com/documentation/guides`,
© OpenCFD Ltd, **CC BY-NC-ND 4.0** — attribution, non-commercial, no
derivatives; both checked 2026-09-09). Neither is Open Source and neither's
terms permit redistributing its text, which is why **no text, figure, table or
dictionary listing from either is reproduced anywhere in this repository**.
What §92 takes from them is four facts and one documented default, restated in
this document's own voice: the stage list (castellation → snapping → layer
addition), the shape of a distance-band refinement specification, the
displacement smoothing in the snapping stage, and the 30-degree feature angle.
The equations of §92.2 come from the papers below or are derived in §92 itself,
and the config key names are deliberately NOT `snappyHexMeshDict`'s (§92 and
the `src/automesher/mod.rs` row below). ANSYS Fluent's *User's Guide* — the
"polyhedra from tetrahedra" conversion §92.5 cites, ANSYS Inc., proprietary
documentation licensed to its users — is read the same way and reproduced the
same amount: nothing.

Gmsh (GPL-2.0-or-later) is **run as an external process** by
`tools/mesh/step_mesh.py`, by `tools/geom/geom_tool.py` (docs/10 §I M1) and, in tranche 2, by §92.4; it is never linked and
its source is never read.

| File | SPEC-LIT | Primary sources |
|---|---|---|
| `src/automesher/mod.rs` | §92.1, §92.2 | **ORIGINAL** — the module's own configuration tree, in the serde + `schemars` shape `src/io/case_json.rs` established, with `deny_unknown_fields` throughout so a mistyped key is refused by name (§13.4). The field set is the stage list of §92.2 and nothing else; no other mesher's dictionary was consulted, and in particular `snappyHexMeshDict` was NOT copied — the names here (`base_size`, `bands`, `keep_region`, `medial_frac`) are this document's |
| `src/automesher/quality.rs` | §92.3, §2.1, §2.2, §2.4 | **ORIGINAL** — the seven-check gate G1–G7 of §92.3 and the refusal that names the cell and its centroid. G1, G2 and G4 measure what `mesh::geometry::check` already measures and G3 calls `mesh::geometry::cell_regions`; what is new is that they are a PRECONDITION on emitting a mesh. **G5**'s dimensionless thickness `tau_c = 3 V_c / A_max^(3/2)`, its reference length (`A_max` is the largest PLANAR FACE GROUP — faces whose outward normals agree to within 5 degrees, summed — so that §74's four coplanar quarter-faces at a 2:1 interface read as the one face they geometrically are) and **G6**'s condition number of the face-area tensor `sum_f Sf Sf^T/|Sf|` with its `1e4` limit are *DESIGN* (§92.3), chosen so the gate needs no octree bookkeeping and runs on a mesh read off disk. G2's `1e-10` is NOT design: it is `mesh::geometry::CLOSURE_LIMIT`, this crate's own, quoted. **Mavriplis, AIAA 2003-3986** (DOI `10.2514/6.2003-3986`) — the conditioning analysis of gradient reconstruction that is why a condition number, rather than a face-area ratio, is the quantity G6 measures. **Knupp, *SIAM J. Sci. Comput.* 23 (2001) 193** (DOI `10.1137/S1064827500371499`) and **Knupp, *Engineering with Computers* 28 (2011) 419** (DOI `10.1007/s00366-011-0230-1`) — what "quality" means in the repair sequence's smoothing step. **Freitag & Ollivier-Gooch, *Int. J. Numer. Meth. Engng* 40 (1997) 3979** (DOI `10.1002/(SICI)1097-0207(19971115)40:21<3979::AID-NME251>3.0.CO;2-9`) — smart Laplacian smoothing, and the "keep the step only if the worst metric improved" rule the repair sequence follows. The symmetric-3x3 eigenvalue closed form is the characteristic polynomial, textbook algebra, from no source |
| `src/automesher/octree.rs` | §92.9, §92.2, §74.2, §2 | **ORIGINAL** — the true octree of §92.9: the packed leaf key (92.17), the ancestor lookup (92.18) that answers "who is across this face" without a tree walk, the emission rule (92.19), the integer-lattice point rule (92.20) and the sparse-leaf restatement (92.21) of §74.2's balance sweep. The 2:1 FACE CONVENTION is not new and must not be — it is `mesh::refined`'s (§74), reused so §74's measured interface constants stay true of these meshes, and the winding of a face is `blockgen::internal_quad`'s, reused so a level-0 tree reproduces `blockgen::raw_mesh` point for point and face for face (a test asserts it, bit for bit). What is this module's own is the decision to weld points by INTEGER identity and never by distance, and (92.22): a leaf whose bounding sphere touches a named patch takes that patch's deepest band level, so a band narrower than the cell cannot hide the surface. **Berger & Colella, *J. Comput. Phys.* 82 (1989) 64** and **Isaac, Burstedde & Ghattas, *IPDPS 2012* 426** (DOI `10.1109/IPDPS.2012.47`) — what a 2:1 interface is, and the O(1)-ripple balance named and deliberately NOT implemented (§74.2's reason: the fixed-point sweep is not the cost at these sizes and is order-independent by construction). No mesher's source was read; snappyHexMesh is GPL and was not opened |
| `src/automesher/castellate.rs` | §92.10, §92.15, §92.2, §23.3, §23.4, §2 | **ORIGINAL** — SPEC-LIT §92.10's stage 3: the centre test (92.23), the removal walk (92.24) with the four-face-edge pinch rule (92.25), and the wall-patch rule (92.26). The classification is `surface::classify`'s §23.3 pipeline, called and not re-implemented; the wall-patch rule and its refusal on a name collision are `blockgen::carve_block`'s (§23.4), reused so a case's boundary conditions attach to the geometry the user named whichever mesher built the mesh; the order argument is §92.9's, inherited through a monotone renumbering rather than repaired by `adapt::rebuild::ldu_permutation`. What is this file's own is (92.25): an edge of the castellated boundary carried by four boundary faces is an hourglass that passes §92.3's gate and that stage 4's snapping would invert, so stage 3 removes one of its two cells, by fewer kept neighbours and then by the larger id so the answer does not depend on visiting order. The point-pinch is NOT repaired and §92.10 says so. No mesher's source was read; snappyHexMesh is GPL and was not opened |
| `src/automesher/snap.rs` | §92.11, §92.15, §92.2, §92.3, §23.4 | **ORIGINAL** — SPEC-LIT §92.11's stage 4: the four point sets (92.27), the dead-banded displacement (92.28), the two Jacobi sweeps (92.29) — (92.6)'s smoothing on the wall's own point graph and a decayed harmonic extension of it into the interior, so the first cell behind a moved wall point does not absorb the whole displacement — the axis constraint (92.30) that holds a point where the geometry meets the box on that box patch's plane, the guarded step and its abandonment (92.31), the area refusal (92.32), and (92.33), which the coding agent found rather than implemented: a hanging node that leaves its parents' segment opens a crack at every 2:1 transition and G2 then fails mesh-wide at any step size, so the trial positions re-seat every hanging node on its parents' midpoint before the gate measures them. §92.11 was amended to state it. **Ericson, *Real-Time Collision Detection*, Morgan Kaufmann (2005) §5.1.5** — the point-to-triangle Voronoi-region test `TriIndex::closest_point` returns the point of, read as a textbook; the projection itself was already in `src/surface/mod.rs` and is called, not copied. **Freitag & Ollivier-Gooch, *Int. J. Numer. Meth. Engng* 40 (1997) 3979** (DOI `10.1002/(SICI)1097-0207(19971115)40:21<3979::AID-NME251>3.0.CO;2-9`) — smart Laplacian smoothing, and why the DISPLACEMENT is smoothed rather than the position. What is this file's own is (92.28)'s dead band (without it a mesh already on its geometry is returned perturbed in the last bits, and the axis-aligned case cannot be asserted bit for bit), (92.30) (the alternative — pinning the point — leaves the staircase the snap exists to remove), (92.31)'s abandonment of a whole iterate rather than acceptance of a partly-moved mesh, which is what makes the loop finite, and (92.32), the one refusal, which measures resolution by AREA because no length scale distinguishes a cavity corner from a resolved one. snappyHexMesh is GPL and was not opened; the OpenFOAM User Guide's prose description of snapping is the only thing consulted about it, and it is cited in §92.2 |
| `src/automesher/features.rs` | §92.12, §92.2, §23.2, §23.4 | **ORIGINAL** — SPEC-LIT §92.12's feature stage: the dihedral edge set (92.34), which counts an OPEN or NON-MANIFOLD edge as a feature because the line where a sheet ends is a line the mesh must hold and there is no second normal to compare there (`Surface::edge_defects`, §23.2, already counts both); the corner rule (92.35), which is degree plus the TURN of the curve, so a polyline never cuts a corner it was supposed to hold; the chaining into polylines between corners, with a corner-free cycle — a cylinder's rim — closed and cut at its lowest point id so the output does not depend on the order the walk discovered edges in; and the segment query (92.36) over a uniform bucket grid whose shape, clamping and memory guard are `surface::TriIndex`'s (§23.4), reused rather than re-derived. The OpenFOAM *User Guide*'s `snappyHexMesh` chapter is cited in §92.2 for its PROSE description of explicit feature snapping — extract the sharp edges as curves, refine to them, snap to them — and that description is the only thing consulted about it; snappyHexMesh is GPL and was not opened. **Marechal, *Proc. 18th International Meshing Roundtable* (2009) 65-84** (DOI `10.1007/978-3-642-04319-2_5`) — sharp-feature handling on an octree hex mesh, and why the refinement criterion is a band about the edge CURVE rather than a curvature test on the triangles. What is this file's own is (92.34)'s second set, (92.35)'s turn test, and the decision that the index answers with the lower edge id on a distance tie, so a linear scan and the bucketed walk agree exactly and a test can assert equality rather than a tolerance |
| `src/automesher/layers.rs` | §92.13, §92.2, §92.3, §92.11 | **ORIGINAL** — SPEC-LIT §92.13, §92.2's stage 6 end to end: the area-weighted point normal (92.40) and its smoothing (92.41), the constraint (92.42) that lets a layer point slide in the plane of every boundary face that is NOT a layer face rather than pinning it — (92.30)'s instrument stated for a general face normal, with the two-constraint case a CROSS PRODUCT and not a second projection, because two planes leave a line and not a plane — the level schedule (92.43), the medial march (92.44), the thickness (92.45), the shrink (92.46), the retreat and patch drop (92.47), the extrusion (92.48), the side faces and their 2:1 splits (92.49), the per-patch report (92.50) and the refusal (92.51) that says `3 t_1 / h` against `quality.min_thickness_ratio` before a cell is inserted, because G5 refuses a layer thinner than `h/60` and the user has to see the trade rather than a gate failure on a cell id. **Garimella & Shephard, *Int. J. Numer. Meth. Engng* 49 (2000) 193-218** (DOI `10.1002/1097-0207(20000910/20)49:1/2<193::AID-NME929>3.0.CO;2-R`) — the inward-extrusion construction and the medial-axis limit that makes it survive a concave corner; the paper is the source of the CONSTRUCTION and of nothing else here. The OpenFOAM *User Guide*'s prose description of layer addition is cited in §92.2 and is the only thing consulted about `snappyHexMesh`, which is GPL and was not opened. What is this file's own is (92.44) — the medial distance measured with `TriIndex::closest_point` alone, as the first `s` at which the closest surface point is both nearer than `(1 - kappa) s` and further than `c s` from where the march started, so that a convex corner (whose closest point is the launch point itself) and a concave corner (whose closest point is `s/sqrt(2)` away) do not read as a second wall, with the 6.7 % over-estimate this costs derived as (92.52); the decision that the layer count is uniform over a PATCH, with the patch dropped and NAMED rather than the stack terminated part-way across it, stated as a limitation in §92.13 and not hidden; the rule that the thickness a point carries is the APPLIED displacement after (92.46)'s hanging line, so a hanging node whose parents lie on a non-layer patch reads as zero and costs its patch the layers rather than extruding a side face of zero area; the segment cut of (92.49), which is built over the BOUNDARY faces here rather than taken from `snap::find_hanging`, whose map keys the hanging NODE and keeps only its longest parent edge; and the measurement §92.13 records of what the stage INHERITS - a wall face's angle against its owner's centre is invisible to G4 until a layer cell turns it into an internal face, and on a snapped sphere 408 of 2592 wall faces are already past 70 degrees, which is why the validation runs on a cube |

It owns only what belongs to no stage — when a run stops, and what the patches of the written case are called — and calls the four stage functions unchanged; the quality report it carries is the LAST stage's own, because every stage of (92.55) gates its own output and re-measuring a million-cell mesh is the expensive part of a run. The rename's three refusals (a key naming no patch, a value the polyMesh reader would refuse, two patches colliding) are this file's, and the patch-name check itself is `io::polymesh::check_patch_name`'s, called and not copied. No mesher's source was read |
| `src/automesher/identity.rs` | §92.14 | **ORIGINAL** — the `identity` block of the run summary (92.57): the deterministic mesh id (FNV-1a 64 over the configured case directory and the mesh name), the run id resolved from `-runId` else `OFGPU_RUN_ID`, and the UTC stamp. FNV-1a is Fowler/Noll/Vo's public-domain hash, implemented from its published two-line definition and its published test vectors; no implementation was read. The civil-date arithmetic is the standard proleptic-Gregorian day-count inverse, textbook algebra, written here because this crate takes no date dependency. No GPL-licensed source was consulted |

The site geometry is the user's own data. matplotlib (PSF-based, BSD-compatible) and numpy (BSD-3) are imported, not vendored |

No GPL-licensed source was consulted |
| `../tools/mesh/examples/recipe.py`, `../tools/mesh/examples/turek_hron.py`, `../tools/mesh/examples/bimetal_strip.py`, `../tools/mesh/examples/thick_cylinder.py`, `../tools/mesh/examples/cantilever.py`, `../tools/mesh/examples/selftest_recipes.py`, `../cases/turekHron/fsi1.jsonc`, `../cases/turekHron/cfd1.jsonc` | — (the region layout is `docs/10-fsi-solid-mesh-plan.md` §C; the benchmark cases are §I M5) | **ORIGINAL** — Gmsh recipes that build the Turek–Hron FSI benchmark channel (2-D OCC fragment, one-cell extrude with recombine → hex) and three solid gate bodies (transfinite hex) and hand the `.msh` to `regions_from_msh.py`; the two case skeletons. Geometry, boundary conditions, material constants and reference values are copied from S. Turek & J. Hron, "Proposal for numerical benchmarking of fluid-structure interaction between an elastic object and laminar incompressible flow", LNCSE 53, Springer (2006) 371–385, DOI 10.1007/3-540-34596-5_15, and its published FEATFLOW tables — a benchmark definition, not code. Gmsh (GPL-2.0-or-later) is run as an external process through its Python API and its source was not read; no OpenFOAM, solids4foam, preCICE or FEATFLOW case file was opened. No GPL-licensed source was consulted |
| `../tools/geom/geom_tool.py`, `../tools/geom/geom_edit.py`, `../tools/geom/stl_repair.py`, `../tools/geom/selftest.py`, `../tools/geom/README.md` | — | **ORIGINAL** — the geometry tool's read side (`docs/10-fsi-solid-mesh-plan.md` §I M1): import by format on gmsh's OpenCASCADE kernel (gmsh is run as a separate program and files are exchanged; its source is never read), the solid list, the export by extension with the millimetre STEP convention of `tools/mesh/diag/export_fluid.py`, and the `<stem>.geom.json` sidecar that carries names and materials because a STEP keeps neither and OCC renumbers tags. The discrete-surface volume is the divergence theorem, `V = Σ a·(b×c)/6`, a textbook identity. `geom_edit.py` (§I M2) is the edit side: fourteen named operations on the same kernel, validated by name before the file is opened; the naming rule that carries a solid's name through OCC's renumbering — gmsh's `out_map` within a run, the sidecar's centroid/volume match across files; and the exact millimetre STEP export by BREP re-import at `Geometry.OCCScaling = 1000`, because `occ.dilate` re-approximates curved faces (probed: 4.4e-4 in volume on a sphere). numpy (BSD-3) is imported, not vendored. `stl_repair.py` (docs/14 T2) repairs an STL to a NEW file and reports what it could not: the weld at `1e-6 x diagonal` is the house convention of `gui/server/src/formats/stl.ts` restated with a KD-tree (scipy, BSD-3) and union-find, its representative a coordinate the file already had; closedness is `src/surface/mod.rs`'s `edge_defects` rule restated in numpy; orientation is propagation across manifold edges, outward by the sign of the divergence-theorem volume; a 3-edge hole is one triangle and a longer boundary loop up to `--max-hole-edges` is ear-clipped in the plane of its Newell normal (Meisters's two-ears theorem: G. H. Meisters, "Polygons have ears", Amer. Math. Monthly 82 (1975) 648–651). The STL format is the 1987 3D Systems de facto public specification (SPEC-LIT §23.1). No mesh-repair library's source was read; gmsh and pymeshlab are not imported |
| `../tools/aero/drag_post.py`, `../tools/aero/fast_patch.py`, `../tools/aero/selftest.py`, `../tools/aero/README.md` | — | **ORIGINAL** — the F1 aero pipeline's two scripts (docs/14 T3), written in the F1 session of 2026-09-17/18 against the OpenFOAM ASCII layout this crate's own writers produce (`src/io/polymesh.rs`, `src/io/fields.rs`, `blockgen.rs`'s dictionaries) and moved under `tools/aero/`: the pressure integral `F_x = Σ p_f Sf_x` over a named wall patch and the momentum-deficit survey `D = Σ ρ u_x (U_∞ − u_x) |A_x|` over a named outlet patch, both textbook control-volume identities, and the fast-mode patch that rewrites a generated case's run settings by regex and counts its hits. Reading the files this crate writes is interoperability, not derivation; no OpenFOAM source was consulted. Pure stdlib. No GPL-licensed source was consulted |
| `../tools/autonomy/schema.py`, `../tools/autonomy/selftest.py`, `../tools/autonomy/README.md`, `../tools/autonomy/gates.json`, `../tools/autonomy/gates.lock`, `../tools/autonomy/schema/flow_spec.schema.json`, `../tools/autonomy/schema/manifest_row.schema.json`, `../tools/autonomy/schema/fingerprint.schema.json`, `../tools/autonomy/schema/decision_record.schema.json`, `../tools/autonomy/schema/attempt_row.schema.json`, `../tools/autonomy/schema/gate_constants.schema.json`, `../tools/autonomy/schema/knobs.schema.json`, `../tools/autonomy/schema/knobs.json`, `../tools/autonomy/fixtures/good.json`, `../tools/deps_licences.py` | — (docs/15 §C, §D, §I-3..I-5) | **ORIGINAL** — AM-1, the autonomous-setup engine's data shapes and its own gate: the seven JSON Schemas (draft 2020-12) for the flow spec, the manifest row, the fingerprint, the DecisionRecord, the `autonomy-attempt/1` attempt row, the gate constants and the knob table; the JSON-Schema-subset validator is the project's own stdlib code, written from the draft-2020-12 keyword list and nothing else (a `bool` is never an `integer`, `enum`/`const` never let `True == 1` pass), with `jsonschema` (MIT) and `referencing` (MIT) imported only inside the selftest as an optional oracle that must agree with it on every good fixture and refuse every mutated one. The §I-3 gate constants sit in `gates.json` locked by `gates.lock`: sha256 over canonical JSON (sort_keys, tight separators, ensure_ascii, allow_nan=False) of the parsed whole file, of each field, and of the knob table, computed identically under different `PYTHONHASHSEED`s and re-checked by `load_gates`/`load_knobs` on every read; relocking is the user's decision (docs/15 §F). The 21-knob whitelist and its legal ranges are read off `rust/src/automesher/mod.rs`'s field lines and its `validate` checks (cited per row), and `check_edit` refuses out-of-action-space edits as DecisionRecords with rule ids `WL-POINTER`/`WL-FORBIDDEN`/`WL-UNLISTED`/`WL-TYPE`/`WL-RANGE` — `/quality`, `/layers/cell_frac`, `/layers/medial_frac` and `-permissive` are forbidden (docs/15 §C, §I-5). `check_attempt` re-derives docs/15 §D's definitions S1–S12 on every row. The a priori y⁺ helpers reproduce §D.3: the flat-plate C_F correlations of SPEC-LIT §32.5.6 (Schlichting & Gersten, *Boundary-Layer Theory*, 8th ed., Springer (2000), as already transcribed in the solver tree — cited, not re-derived), `u_tau = U·sqrt(C_F/2)`, `t1 = y⁺·nu/u_tau`, worked example L = 1 m, ν = 1.5e-5 m²/s, Re_L = 2e4 → t1 = 7.297e-4 m, and every y⁺-bearing name must carry `_a_priori` until AR-2 compares it with a solved y⁺ (docs/15 §I-4). `README.md` carries docs/15 §D verbatim between markers. `deps_licences.py` grows a `--python` branch that reads `importlib.metadata` only and reports declared/detected licences and bundled components of numpy, scipy, scikit-learn (all detected BSD-3-Clause; their wheels' GCC runtime library carries GPL-3.0-with-GCC-exception, listed as a NOTE) and the optional jsonschema/referencing (MIT); the cargo path is untouched. `tools/mesh/selftest.py` runs `tools/autonomy/selftest.py` as its last check. No GPL-licensed source was consulted |

| `../tools/cad/common.py`, `../tools/cad/schema.py`, `../tools/cad/selftest.py`, `../tools/cad/fixtures/digest20.json`, `../tools/cad/fixtures/validator/probe.schema.json`, `../tools/cad/fixtures/validator/cases.json` | — (docs/16 §I CAD-01, §D Reproducibility) | **ORIGINAL** — CAD-01, the deterministic foundation of the CAD loop. `common.py` defines the one canonical JSON form (sort_keys, tight separators, ensure_ascii, allow_nan False) whose sha256 is the reproducibility key: the form is identical across hash seeds, proved in-process and in two fresh PYTHONHASHSEED children, and pinned by a hardcoded digest over a 20-key fixture; beside it sit atomic writes through a same-directory temp file and `os.replace` (with a bounded PermissionError retry, and every failure path removing the temp and leaving the old file byte-identical), an fsynced jsonl appender whose reader names the line of any truncated tail, and an environment fingerprint read from `importlib.metadata` only — no package is imported — with `occt` derived from the cadquery-ocp version's first three dot components. `schema.py` is a stdlib JSON-Schema draft 2020-12 subset validator of exactly 23 keywords with local `$ref` only and no oneOf/anyOf/allOf, written on the pattern of this repository's own `tools/autonomy/schema.py` at e63c61f (same copyright holder, original code), its error strings the autonomy file's, `jsonschema` (MIT) used only as an optional selftest oracle that must agree on the 12 probe fixtures. `selftest.py` is the gate: it runs each module's `--selftest` as a child, requires the [ok] minimum and a SELFTEST PASS line, exits non-zero on any miss, and proves it can refuse by failing against three broken fake children. No GPL-licensed source was consulted |
| `../tools/cad/schema/` (the nine *.schema.json), `../tools/cad/fixtures/schema/` (the nine *.cases.json), `../tools/cad/schema_fixtures.py` | — (docs/16 §I CAD-02, §E.1, §E.4, §E.6-§E.7, §G, §H.1) | **ORIGINAL** — CAD-02, the record schemas of the CAD loop: nine flat JSON-Schema draft 2020-12 documents, one per record the loop writes (the frozen template declaration, the parameter vector, the approved and locked requirement set, the compiled checks, one primitive measurement, one evaluation's verdicts, one iterations.jsonl row without a timestamp so an interrupted and an uninterrupted run agree byte for byte, one decision, the locked gate constants). They carry no union keyword because GLM sends empty arguments for union tools; instead of optional fields every field is required and the absent value is null; inline objects are closed (additionalProperties false, required the property list in order); the only reusable pieces are local $defs copied from one shared sixteen-entry table, so each file stands on its own. Each kind's supervisor-written case file holds a valid instance plus single-field mutations, and the CAD-01 validator must pass the instance and refuse every mutation with exactly the case's own error, whose first string names the mutated field, with `jsonschema` (MIT) agreeing on all instances as an optional selftest oracle. A flatness check refuses any schema that breaks that shape, proved against five planted non-flat schemas, and the harness runs all of it as its third child. No GPL-licensed source was consulted |
| `../tools/cad/measure.py` | — (docs/16 §I CAD-03, §E.2, §H.3 GC-1, §C) | **ORIGINAL** — CAD-03, the typed measurement primitives of the CAD loop. Each of twelve primitives (cylinder radius, cone semi-angle, volume, diameter at a named plane, area ratio, axial extent, plane distance, meridian minimum wall, maximum slope, minimum curvature radius, solid count, validity) returns one `cad-measure/1` record with its unit, its uncertainty from docs/16 §E.2 and a fixed method string, and never raises: a method that cannot give the defined quantity answers by rule id with a null value. Diameters are exact only where the section is a circle on the axis, extents come from BRepExtrema distances to far planes rather than a bounding box, and wall thickness is the minimum distance between the 2-D meridian edges tagged wetted and outer, cross-checked by dense polyline sampling; the 3-D surface-to-surface wall distance is refused by name (`MEAS-3D-WALL`). The selftest checks every primitive against analytic answers (cylinder, frustum, cone band with normal and radial offsets, Bell & Mehta's fifth-order contraction, a torus, a circular arc) and records that the plan's 3.000 mm 3-D false pass came from a probe that selected only the exit-tube line, while full generators read the true 1.8759 mm. Uses CadQuery (Apache-2.0) and its OCP bindings of OCCT (LGPL-2.1, used as a library, no source consulted), numpy and scipy (BSD). No GPL-licensed source was consulted |
| `../tools/cad/runner.py`, `../tools/cad/child.py`, `../tools/cad/hints.py`, `../tools/cad/fixtures/runner/` (8 fixture scripts) | — (docs/16 §I CAD-04, §A rows 56-57, 72 and 74, §D S3, §F) | **ORIGINAL**, except the lines of `hints.py` between its VERBATIM markers — CAD-04, the killable child of the CAD loop. `runner.py` writes a job file, spawns `child.py` as a separate Python process with files (never pipes) for its output, and on timeout kills the whole PID tree (`taskkill /T /F /PID` on Windows, the process group on POSIX), never by image name or command line; it maps exit codes to ok, error or crash, naming Windows NTSTATUS values from Microsoft [MS-ERREF] §2.3.1 as well as POSIX signals and shell 128+n codes, keeps the last 4 KB of the child's stderr, refuses a malformed job before spawning, never reads a stale result, and never imports the module it runs. `child.py` is the only process that loads a module, by path through `importlib`, with faulthandler on and the Windows crash dialogs suppressed. The idea of subprocess isolation is ai-cad's (`execute_in_subprocess`, idea only, reimplemented); its POSIX-only crash detection is why NTSTATUS is here, and the selftest records that the plan's `ctypes.string_at(0)` fixture is an OSError on Windows, not a crash. **`hints.py` between the markers is ai-cad-labs/ai-cad at c7503b4, `.shared/tools/cadquery_executor.py` lines 93-146 (the `_HINTS` table and `match_hint()`), Apache-2.0, copied byte-verbatim and pinned by sha256**, with AI-CAD's attribution, the Apache-2.0 notice and the mark "modified by Iteration-CFD (Iterations Co., Ltd.)" in its header (NOTICE, `LICENSE-APACHE-2.0.ai-cad`, decision D-5). No file under tools/cad contains an exec or eval call, checked by the selftest. Standard library only. No GPL-licensed source was consulted |
| `../tools/cad/templates/nozzle_contraction/template.py`, `../tools/cad/templates/nozzle_contraction/template.json`, `../tools/cad/template_gc3.py`, `../tools/cad/fixtures/template/prf_inject.py` | Bell & Mehta, NASA CR-177488 (1988), US Government work, https://ntrs.nasa.gov/api/citations/19890004382/downloads/19890004382.pdf; Morel, J. Fluids Eng. 97(2):225-233 (1975), DOI 10.1115/1.3447255 (docs/16 §I CAD-05, §H.1, §H.3 GC-3, §G) | **ORIGINAL** — CAD-05, the first frozen-template candidate of the CAD loop: an axisymmetric subsonic contraction revolved on +x. Its wall laws are Bell & Mehta's 3rd-, 5th- and 7th-order polynomials, each built as an exact Bezier curve from its Bernstein coefficients, and Morel's two matched cubics (the published law only; no chart transcribed). The body's outer wall is the true normal offset of the wetted curve: an exact offset curve, with any swallowtail loop found by the distance to the wetted curve and cut by bisection, each kept piece re-fitted as a B-spline within 1e-11 m so that STEP keeps the body a solid. Inputs are refused by rule id before any 3-D operation (PRF-BOX, PRF-RMIN, PRF-MONO, PRF-DERIV, PRF-SELFX, PRF-FACE2D); tags are assigned geometrically and survive a BREP reload. `template.json` is generated from the module's declaration and validates as cad-template/1. The module imports only math, cadquery and OCP, as docs/16 §G's admission requires. `template_gc3.py` proves gate GC-3 through runner children only. No GPL-licensed source was consulted |
| `../tools/cad/export.py`; edits to `../tools/cad/measure.py` (primitives `watertight`, `axis_x`, `units_m`) and `../tools/cad/templates/nozzle_contraction/template.py` (one shared vertex per meridian junction; three catalogue rows), `../tools/cad/templates/nozzle_contraction/template.json` (regenerated) | — (docs/16 §I CAD-06, §D S3, §E.2, §H.3 GC-4 and GC-5) | **ORIGINAL** — CAD-06, the S3 export of the CAD loop. `export.py` writes the fluid, body and meridian STEP through OCCT's STEP writer with `write.step.unit` M, so each file declares METRE over its metre values (CadQuery's default declares MILLI.METRE over the same numbers, a silent 1000x), and fixes the header so the bytes repeat; a named-patch ASCII STL from one `BRepMesh_IncrementalMesh` with reversed faces flipped; `tags.json`, `probes.json` (every catalogue row measured as a cad-measure/1 record) and `geom.json` (units m, scale 1, axis +x, per-tag area and type, template and params sha, environment). `tools/geom/stl_repair.py --weld 0` is the watertightness oracle, and gmsh, in a fresh process, reads the STEP at OCCTargetUnit M to prove the span. The template now builds every meridian junction on one shared vertex, because two vertices a few ulp apart were merged in a process-dependent order and made the BREP bytes differ between runs. The selftest proves GC-5, GC-4 (12 files byte-identical across two fresh processes) and that each new check can fail. No GPL-licensed source was consulted |
| `../tools/cad/readiness.py` | — (docs/16 §I CAD-09, §D S5, §C facts 1 and 4, §E.5) | **ORIGINAL** — CAD-09, the S5 readiness stage of the CAD loop. It judges the files one S3 export wrote, not the template's parameters, and refuses by the first failing rule in a fixed order: RDY-PROFILE (the meridian is one planar face in z = 0, never below the axis, its wall edges strictly off it, no self-intersection, BRepCheck valid), RDY-BREP (the fluid is valid by BRepCheck and BOPCheck, exactly one solid, no free wires), RDY-FACEW (every fluid face has 2A/P at least the mesh size h), RDY-EDGE (every fluid and meridian edge at least h/2), RDY-STL (the stl_repair --weld 0 report is the one geom.json hashed, watertight, agrees with geom.json and within the STL volume tolerance), RDY-THROAT (the smallest wall diameter at least 20 h) and RDY-TAGS (every declared face tag, the axis and the planes present once and partitioning the faces and meridian edges). A missing input refuses and an unreadable one is an error; neither is ever ready. The selftest proves that the planner's axis-crossing and a bow-tie profile, both of which revolve into BRepCheck-valid solids, are refused by RDY-PROFILE, a 0.05 mm lip by RDY-FACEW and a missing inlet face by RDY-BREP, that each later rule refuses a planted defect, and that the nominal is ready. No GPL-licensed source was consulted |

Densities are fixed at 293.15 K and 101 325 Pa: water 998.21 kg/m3 (IAPWS-95 as tabulated by the NIST Chemistry WebBook, https://webbook.nist.gov/chemistry/fluid/), air 1.2041 kg/m3 (p/(RT), R = 287.05 J/(kg K)). The fixtures are 20 supervisor-written sets (8 accepted, 12 each refused by exactly one id) and 8 golden compiles. No GPL-licensed source was consulted |
| `../tools/cad/verify.py`; `../tools/cad/fixtures/verify/` | ai-cad-labs/ai-cad `c7503b4` (Apache-2.0), the idea only: a tri-state verdict with a deterministically derived overall verdict (`dfma_evaluator.py:829-843`, ADR 0009 #5); no code copied (docs/16 §A, §I CAD-08, §D S4 and S10, §E.4) | **ORIGINAL** — CAD-08, the verdict rule of the CAD loop. Each compiled cad-checks/1 assertion and its cad-measure/1 record become pass, fail or not_evaluable from the margin against the uncertainty u: for `<=` b, pass iff m + u <= b + tol and fail iff m - u > b + tol, `>=` mirrored, `==` on abs(m - v), `in` as the conjunction, otherwise NE-UNCERTAIN. u is the larger of the compiled u and the record's u_meas; a check compiled with u null (a CFD row such as SYS-MACH) takes u = max(GCI_fine, repeat band) and is NE-UNCERTAIN when neither is given, never u = 0. A missing or refused measurement is NE-MISSING, a raised one or a non-finite value NE-ERROR, and neither ever passes; is_true primitives have no uncertainty; objective rows carry m and u for the promotion gate. The design verdict is derived from the hard rows only: any fail infeasible, all pass feasible, otherwise not_evaluable. The fixture is 30 supervisor-written table cases in dyadic numbers, so each boundary equality is exact in binary floating point. No GPL-licensed source was consulted |

Each mutant is measured by the production export and judged by `verify.evaluate` against a supervisor-written requirement set that `reqs.py` accepts and compiles; a mutant is killed only by a hard geometric row whose verdict is fail, never by a not-evaluable row or by readiness, which is recorded beside it. The selftest proves 10 of 10 killed with the exact kill sets, every one of the 11 hard geometric rows killing at least one, the nominal passing all 13 geometric rows, an identity control that changes no measurement, and that the coverage check fails when the only killers of a row are removed. The selftest proves GC-6 at 10,140 / 40,560 / 162,240 cells, byte-identical MSH files from two fresh processes, that each gate predicate and the binary's own gate can fail, and five refusals by id. `bin.json` records each binary's path and sha256 and is refused on a mismatch. No GPL-licensed source was consulted |
| `../tools/cad/thwaites.py` | Thwaites 1949, Aeronaut. Q. 1(3):245-280, DOI 10.1017/S0001925900000184; Rott & Crabtree 1952, DOI 10.2514/8.2381; the Cebeci & Bradshaw (1977) H(lambda), l(lambda) fits as restated in White, Viscous Fluid Flow, sec. 4-6; Savitzky & Golay 1964, DOI 10.1021/ac60214a047 (formulas cited and reimplemented, no code) (docs/16 §I CAD-12, §D S9, §H.4 G2) | **ORIGINAL** — CAD-12, the laminar boundary-layer reference of the CAD loop. `solve` integrates theta^2 r^2 U^6 = theta0^2 r0^2 U0^6 + 0.45 nu integral(r^2 U^5 dx) by a cumulative trapezoid on the given stations, takes dU/dx from a fixed 11-station, cubic Savitzky-Golay fit generalised to a nonuniform grid, clamps lambda = theta^2 U'/nu to [-0.09, 0.25] with a flag, and returns theta, delta* = H theta, Cf = 2 nu l/(U theta), Re_theta and a separation flag at Cf <= 0.0005; a stagnation or axis start is undefined (NaN) at station 0 only. Bad inputs are refused by eight THW-* ids, and `solve IN OUT` writes canonical JSON with NaN as null. The selftest proves the flat plate sqrt(0.45), Hiemenz lambda 0.075 and Homann lambda 0.05625 to 1e-4, the planar limit at constant radius, a restart from theta0, the window's locality, Howarth's separation at x/L 0.123, the plan's nozzle numbers (theta_e/R_e 0.0059 and 0.0102 within 3 %, slope -0.503 +- 0.01) and that three planted mutants of the method fail. No GPL-licensed source was consulted |
| `../tools/cad/turb_integral.py` | Schlichting, NACA TM 1218 (1949) eqs. 16.4, 16.14, 16.22, 16.26, 17.3-17.11, https://ntrs.nasa.gov/citations/20050040758; Nikuradse, NASA TT F-10,359 (1966), https://archive.org/details/nasa_techdoc_19670004508; Rott & Crabtree 1952, DOI 10.2514/8.2381; Head, ARC R&M 3152 (1958), https://reports.aerade.cranfield.ac.uk/handle/1826.2/3720 (Figs. 1-2 digitised as data from the scan, sha256 5317c6fc...1eb0); Ludwieg & Tillmann, NACA TM 1285 (1950), https://ntrs.nasa.gov/citations/19930093945; the Cebeci & Bradshaw (1977) fits of Head's curves (cited, comparison only); Kline et al. 1967 DOI 10.1017/S0022112067001740 (cited) as restated in arXiv:2306.05972 §3.1 (formulas cited and reimplemented, no code) (docs/16 §I CAD-24, §H.5, §H.3 GC-8) | **ORIGINAL** — CAD-24, the turbulent references of the CAD loop. `solve` integrates the axisymmetric 1/7-power momentum integral d(theta^(5/4))/dx + (5/4) theta^(5/4) [(2 + 9/7) U'/U + r'/r] = (5/4) c (nu/U)^(1/4) as one quadrature with the integrating factor (U^(2+H) r)^(5/4), normalised at the last station so a constant radius is planar bit for bit, with c set so the plate limit is theta = 0.037 x Re_x^(-1/5); it returns theta, delta*, delta, Cf, u_tau and the acceleration parameters K = nu U'/U^2 and p = -nu U U'/u_tau^3. The module also holds Blasius (16.4), Prandtl-Nikuradse (16.26) in closed form from Re_tau, the log law (16.14), the core defect 4.07 (16.22), the TG0 body-force drive, and `k_max_1d`, the exact maximum of K Re_De on the 1-D area rule and the closed-form shortest L/D_i at K 3e-6. Head's entrainment method with Ludwieg-Tillmann friction runs REPORT-ONLY on the digitised curves (or the fits), counting the stations outside them. The selftest proves GC-8 (plate 0.037 to 1e-6, planar limit exact, slope -0.2 to 1e-6, f 0.02579 / 0.01802, Blasius 0.026606, K_max Re_De 5.454 / 0.895, shortest L/D_i 0.5704 / 0.7107 / 0.8282) and that four planted mutants fail. The lock is anchored outside the study: the genesis row of iterations.jsonl and the append-only studies.jsonl, so rewriting requirements.json together with requirements.lock fools only the sibling check. §E.7's ladder stops on max_evals or feasible-then-stop, turns the LLM off after llm_reject_k rejected edits and consults it after stall_k non-promotions; the L2 confirmation passes only when every hard repr-cfd margin strictly exceeds max(GCI_fine, repeat band). Gate: the 16 scenario tables plus 6 - a missing verdict, an extra verdict, a tampered evidence file, an evidence file changed during comparison, a stale base key, and both lock files rewritten consistently. A scrambled Sobol pool of 256 points per law (seed crc32(study_id + law)) over the template's active box; a CAD-only prefilter through the runner (the build, every hard brep/stl row judged by verify.judge, readiness); one Matern-2.5 GP per hard mesh/cfd row on the margin (normalize_y, fixed random_state; features: the searched reals scaled to [0, 1] and a one-hot law); an initial design of the first passed candidate of each law in turn, then the probability of feasibility until the stable design is feasible, then the improvement on the exactly known objective times that probability; ties to the lower (law, Sobol) index; dedup by params sha. Abstains CADOPT-NOFEAS (nothing passes the prefilter, or the best probability of feasibility is below 1e-3), CADOPT-GPFIT (fewer than 3 trainable points, or a leave-one-out z RMS above 10) and CADOPT-EXHAUSTED as cad-decision/1 rows carrying requirements_lock, base_stable_eval_key and gate.deltas. ONE deterministic walk shared by `run`, `intake` and `replay` re-derives every row: a row already on disk must equal what the walk derives again (first_mismatch `<file>:<index>`), the frontier is where it appends. Every evaluation is built under `tmp/<eval_key>/` and moved whole by ONE `os.replace` into `cache/<eval_key>/`, whose six files (eval.json, verdict.json, params.json, measurements.json, geom.json, cfd_u.json) are validated on every use (exact file set, stable_file_snapshot re-hashes, the verdict recomputed equal) and never rewritten after, so a closed study's run rewrites no byte and changes no mtime. The stable pointer is the single file `params/stable.json`, replaced atomically; each promoted-over pointer is backed up write-once under `history/<gate row n>/stable.json`; a kill at any of the counted kill points (exit 77, mid-action) leaves a resume that produces byte-identical iterations.jsonl, decisions.jsonl and stable.json. The intake verifies in INTAKE_IDS order (CAD-EDIT, CAD-LOCKED, GATE-STALE, CAD-INTENT, CAD-RANGE), stores the edit file write-once by its content sha under `intake/`, records a refusal as a reject row rather than an exception, turns the LLM off after llm_reject_k rejected edits, and runs only at rest points (LOOP-BUSY otherwise). The replay re-derives every record including the 1024 prefilter rows and each cache verdict (mismatch `cache:<key>`). Refusals by id: LOOP-STUDY, LOOP-TEMPLATE, LOOP-EVALUATOR, LOOP-IMMUTABLE, LOOP-LINEAGE, LOOP-CHECKS, LOOP-PREFILTER, LOOP-CACHE, LOOP-RESUME, LOOP-BUSY, LOOP-CLOSED, LOOP-MAXEVALS, GATE-LOCK (through the anchor), GATE-STALE, CAD-EDIT, CAD-LOCKED, CAD-INTENT, CAD-RANGE, GATE-LLMOFF. The papers §92.2 and §92.4–§92.6 cite for the stages **not yet implemented** —
Schneiders (1996, DOI `10.1007/BF01198732`); Ito, Shih & Soni (2009, DOI
`10.1002/nme.2470`); Marechal (2009, DOI `10.1007/978-3-642-04319-2_5`); Owen,
Staten & Sorensen (2011, DOI `10.1007/978-3-642-24734-7_9`); Isaac, Burstedde &
Ghattas (2012, DOI `10.1109/IPDPS.2012.47`, already §74's); Garimella & Shephard
(2000, DOI `10.1002/1097-0207(20000910/20)49:1/2<193::AID-NME929>3.0.CO;2-R`);
Cheng et al. (2000, DOI `10.1145/355483.355487`); Peric, *ERCOFTAC Bulletin* 62
(2004), "Flow Simulation Using Control Volumes of Arbitrary Polyhedral Shape"
(the bulletin publishes no page numbers; the article, author, bulletin and year
are confirmed, the page range is not and is not quoted); Geuzaine & Remacle (2009, DOI `10.1002/nme.2579`); Hu et al. (2020,
DOI `10.1145/3386569.3392385`, fTetWild, **MPL-2.0 and therefore readable — not
yet read**); Karras, *HPG 2012* 33-37 (Eurographics Digital Library, DOI
`10.2312/EGGH/HPG12/033-037`, which resolves through **DataCite, not
Crossref**); Cao, Nanjappa, Gao & Tan (2014, DOI `10.1145/2556700.2556710`) — are
listed here so the record is complete, and **none of them has been implemented
yet**. Every DOI in this block was checked against Crossref on 2026-09-09 by
fetching `api.crossref.org/works/<doi>` and comparing title, container and year;
Karras (2012) is not indexed by Crossref — its DOI is a DataCite one and is
quoted as such, and its page range is the Eurographics record's 33-37.

## Case format interoperability — format

Written from the format as it appears in data files, so that existing pre- and
post-processing tools (ParaView, `foamToVTK`) work with ofgpu output. ofgpu
links against no part of OpenFOAM and contains no OpenFOAM source.

| File | What it reads or writes |
|---|---|
| `src/io/tokenizer.rs` | The ASCII tokeniser |
| `src/io/dict.rs` | Dictionaries, `#include`, `$var` lookup, `Switch`, dimensioned scalars |
| `src/io/polymesh.rs` | `constant/polyMesh`: points, faces, owner, neighbour, boundary |
| `src/io/fields.rs` | `volScalarField` / `volVectorField` files and their patch entries |
| `src/io/case.rs` | `system/fvSolution`, `system/fvSchemes`, `system/controlDict`, `constant/*` |
| `src/io/regex.rs` | The pattern matcher for `boundaryField` and `solvers` keys. Written from scratch — a backtracking matcher over the subset the format uses, anchored at both ends. No regex crate is a dependency |
| `src/io/contract.rs` | The unsupported-setting contract of SPEC-LIT §13.4 |
| `src/io/regex.rs` | The POSIX ERE matcher a quoted dictionary key needs. Written from IEEE Std 1003.1 §9.4, Kleene (1956) and Thompson (1968) |
| `src/io/contract.rs` | SPEC-LIT §13.4: a setting this solver cannot honour fails loudly, and `-permissive` is the one escape hatch. Ours by design |

The `FoamFile` banner these write is ofgpu's own, and states plainly that the
file is written by ofgpu in that format. It is not the upstream banner.

Nothing imported or linked |

Files added by AMG-0: `../docs/16a-amagine-borrow.md` (ORIGINAL, the addendum) and `../LICENSE-APACHE-2.0.amagine3d` (their LICENSE blob byte for byte, sha256 cfc7749b96f63bd31c3c42b5c471bf756814053e847c10f3eb003417bc523d30); their NOTICE's copyright lines are in `../NOTICE`, its build123d paragraph is not carried because nothing build123d-derived is copied. build123d stays unused (D-11); manifold3d and lib3mf are not added. Nothing imported or linked. No GPL-licensed source was consulted |
| `../reference/fds` | US Government work (NIST), public domain | The low-Mach formulation of Rehm & Baum (1978), the FDS User's Guide's mesh-resolution arithmetic, and the public-domain HVAC and hose-stream validation decks. Any file that adapts from it says so and acknowledges NIST. **This is a path into a LOCAL clone: this repository does not carry `reference/fds`.** `.gitignore` tracks only `reference/PROVENANCE.md` and `reference/ghia1982/` under `reference/`, and `NOTICE` says the same; where a published FDS number is held against, it is a transcribed literal named in `reference/PROVENANCE.md` |

Every citation is inline at the byte it explains. No source copied; nothing linked. The NanoVDB layout in `src/io/nvdb.rs` comes from the same project's Apache-2.0 PNanoVDB/NanoVDB headers |

## Validation

`SPEC-LIT.md` §10 and §22 are the list, and the rule is absolute: **no test
compares against another CFD code.** Method of manufactured solutions,
analytical solutions, and published benchmark data — Ghia, Ghia & Shin (1982);
Moser, Kim & Mansour (1999); Driver & Seegmiller (1985); McCaffrey (1979);
Martin & Moyce (1952).

One note on the last of those. `ofgpu-vof -surge` and the `#[ignore]`d
`a_dam_break_surge_front_stays_under_the_ritter_bound` in `src/vof.rs` print
the surge front in Martin & Moyce's dimensionless variables so that a reader
holding the paper can lay one on the other. **Their table is deliberately not
transcribed into this repository**, because nobody working on it has the paper
to transcribe from, and a table of numbers attributed to a 1952 experiment and
actually recalled would be worse than no table. What those tests *assert* is
analytic: exact boundedness of `alpha`, exact conservation of the phase volume,
and a surge front that never outruns the Ritter (1892) shallow-water
characteristic speed `2 sqrt(g h0)`.

Agreement with another solver to 1e-13 is not evidence that this code is
correct. It is evidence of something else entirely, and it is exactly what this
project spent a phase removing.
